# 1.6.0 — THE MAP PROOF: is the map EXHAUSTIVE?

> **What this document is.** The owner asked for two things: *"1) map everything 2) tell me you
> mapped everything and prove it."* This is (2). It is not another list of work. It is the
> **audit of the map's completeness** — the document that decides whether the sentence "nothing is
> unlisted" can be defended.
>
> **The verdict is at the bottom, and it is INCOMPLETE.** Read §12 first if you only read one thing.

---

## 0. THE METHOD — count-in = count-out, per source

For every place work can be recorded, three numbers:

| | meaning |
|---|---|
| **COUNT IN** | what the source mechanically contains, extracted by a command printed in this file |
| **COUNT OUT** | how many of those reached **the master list** (defined in §1) as a row somebody owns |
| **DELTA** | `IN − OUT`. A non-zero delta is either an extraction bug or a dropped item. Finding those is the entire point. |

**A delta is not a failure to be hidden. An unexplained delta is.** Every non-zero delta below is
either reconciled in prose or named as unreconciled. Nothing is rounded, nothing is asserted from
memory, and every number carries the command that produces it.

### 0.1 THE PIN, and why it matters more than usual

Everything here is measured at:

```
PIN = 016422be7904d9ede08c99dd78de855613b3da16
```

**This tree is a shared checkout with ~12 concurrent agents and it moved under this measurement.**
That is not a caveat, it is a finding — see §9.2. Two source documents were present on disk at the
start of this audit and deleted from the working tree before it finished, which changed a banned-word
count by 10 occurrences mid-run. **Every number below is therefore read from the git object store at
`PIN`, never from the working tree**, e.g.:

```sh
git show 016422be7:docs/design/BUSBAR-1.6.0.md | ...
```

A number you cannot reproduce at `PIN` is a bug in this document. Say so.

---

## 1. THE MASTER LIST — what "COUNT OUT" is measured against

Before anything can be reconciled, the thing being reconciled *to* has to exist and be named. At
`PIN` there is **no single file that is the map.** `docs/design/1.6.0-map.md` does not exist. So the
master list is the union of the two artefacts that actually carry owned rows:

> **⛔ THIS TABLE IS A `PIN`-ERA OBJECT AND ONE OF ITS FOUR COMPONENTS NO LONGER EXISTS. READ §1.2
> BEFORE USING IT AS A DENOMINATOR.** **M4 is ABSENT from HEAD** — folded into `1.6.0-LEDGER.md`
> and deleted at `b3643b73d`. Two components that now carry **510 rows between them**
> (`1.6.0-LEDGER.md`, `1.6.0-MAP.md`) did not exist at `PIN` and are not in this table, so the
> sentence above — *"no single file that is the map"* — is **true at `PIN` and false today**.
> **A master list with a missing component cannot serve as anyone's denominator**, and three of
> the four rows below are **sections of `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md`**, which is also S1's source. §1.2 and
> Appendix H.

| # | master-list component | location at PIN | rows | of which OPEN work |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | **THE MAP — what stands between here and done** | `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 7, `## THE MAP`, `### 1.`–`### 6.` | 6 | 6 |
| M2 | **OWNER DECISIONS** numbered items | `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 7, `## OWNER DECISIONS`, items 1–20 | 20 | **15** (5 struck `~~…~~ RULED`) |
| M3 | **Part 7 narrative sections** | `### 11.` export plugins · `### 12.` oracle blindness · `### 13.` a2a PARKED | 3 | 3 |
| M4 | **The owner's decision list** | `docs/design/1.6.0-owner-decisions.md` `## 1.`–`## 21.` | 21 | 21 |
| | **MASTER LIST TOTAL** | | **50** | **45** |

Reproduce M1/M3:

```sh
git show 016422be7:docs/design/BUSBAR-1.6.0.md | sed -n '1099,1200p' | grep -nE '^#{2,4} '
```

Reproduce M2 (5 struck vs 15 open):

```sh
git show 016422be7:docs/design/BUSBAR-1.6.0.md | sed -n '1176,1740p' \
  | grep -cE '^\s{0,3}[0-9]{1,2}\. ~~'      # -> 5 struck
```

Reproduce M4:

```sh
git show 016422be7:docs/design/1.6.0-owner-decisions.md | grep -cE '^## [0-9]+\. '   # -> 21
```

### 1.1 THE MASTER LIST HAS THREE DEFECTS OF ITS OWN

A completeness proof that does not audit its own baseline is worthless. Three, all measured:

1. **M2 item 4 and M2 item 8 are the same subject, and only one is struck.** Item 8
   (`busbar-core-substrate`) reads `~~…~~ RULED 2026-09-22 → SPLIT (#83a). The roster becomes 34.`
   Item 4 (`busbar-core-substrate` — *"kept in the roster on the owner's word"*) is **not struck and
   still reads as open**. An agent scanning the open set re-opens a settled ruling. This is the
   documented failure mode #15/#58 exist to prevent.
2. **M2 item 9 and M2 item 14 are the same finding, counted twice.** Both are
   *"#81a(f) — a fractional count bound for an ABI-2 store; four `i64` columns cannot hold it."*
   The open count of 15 is therefore **14 distinct items**, and any burndown keyed on it will report
   a phantom.
3. **M4 item 1 was ruled while M4 was being written.** `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md` §1 asks *"Is
   `cpufeatures -> libc` an I/O reach?"*. It was committed at `c6f69b6d4` (16:22) and **ruled at
   `b106a13f2`/`a197ad2f5` (16:24–16:28)** — with the ruling text landing in the spec at
   `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md:2473` (*"OWNER RULING 2026-09-22 — `cpufeatures -> libc` IS NOT AN I/O REACH
   (settles the row below)"*). M4's own preamble says *"Anything already ruled"* is excluded
   deliberately. **It is not excluded.** M4's live open count is 20, not 21.

**Corrected master list: 50 rows, 45 nominally open, 42 distinct-and-genuinely-open.**

**A FOURTH DEFECT, and it is a *reference* defect rather than a counting one: M2 and M3 collide on
three numbers.** M3 is `### 11.` / `### 12.` / `### 13.`; M2 is *"items 1–20"*, which contains items
11, 12 and 13 — **and they are six different subjects.** M2 item 12 is *"TWO CUSTOMER-VISIBLE DRIFTS
ON THE HOOK KIND"*; M3 `### 12.` is *"THE ORACLE IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO PLANE MONEY"*. **A citation
of the form *"M3 §12"* or *"OWNER DECISION 12"* resolves to two rows in one document**, and §3's own
bucket-F table uses exactly that form. The 50 is right; the pointers are ambiguous (App. H.1.2).

against is not the one §1's table describes.**

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** | `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 7 `## THE MAP` `### 1.`–`### 6.` | present | **PRESENT** | **6** |
| **M2** | `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 7 `## OWNER DECISIONS` items 1–20 | present | **PRESENT** | **20** (5 struck) |
| **M3** | `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` Part 7 `### 11.` · `### 12.` · `### 13.` | present | **PRESENT** | **3** |
| **M4** | `docs/design/1.6.0-owner-decisions.md` `## 1.`–`## 21.` | present | ⛔ **ABSENT** (`b3643b73d`) | **0** |
| **M5** | `docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md` — born `3d822e503`, 2026-09-22 18:21 | ⛔ absent | **PRESENT** | **373** (`R1`–`R78` contiguous + `G`/`M`/`S`/`T`/`ENG`/`DOC`/`GS`/`RB`/`BR-*`) |

```sh
N=72c9c14d62e2e1ad0f56bfbd9c4c23d022693aa8
for p in docs/design/1.6.0-owner-decisions.md docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md docs/design/1.6.0-MAP.md; do

done      # -> ABSENT · PRESENT · PRESENT

**M4 was folded, not lost.** `b3643b73d` (*"ledger: the 22 owner decisions…"*) moved it into

own numbering so any row traces back"* — **28 rows over 22 distinct source numbers**, all 21 of M4's
items among them. **M4's slot in the master list is held by that section**, and any citation of the
form *"M4 §n"* (§3's bucket-F table carries four) must be repointed there.

**Two of the six components are sections of `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md`'s Part 7 — three counting M1 — which
is simultaneously the master list and S1's source.** That is the finding Appendix H turns on.

**THE HONEST TALLY — ~~`15 of 15`~~ → `11 of 15`. IT IS STATED ONCE, HERE, AND IT IS DERIVED BY
COMMAND, NOT INHERITED.**

```sh
# the denominator: how many SOURCE rows does §2 actually have?
awk '/^## 2\. THE RECONCILIATION TABLE/{f=1} /^\*\*THE HONEST TALLY/{f=0} f' \
  docs/design/1.6.0-map-proof.md | grep -cE '^\| S'                          # -> 15

# the numerator: how many of them carry a ✅ in the STATUS cell (the LAST cell of the row)?
awk '/^## 2\. THE RECONCILIATION TABLE/{f=1} /^\*\*THE HONEST TALLY/{f=0} f' \
  docs/design/1.6.0-map-proof.md | awk -F'|' '/^\| S/{print $(NF-1)}' | grep -c '✅'   # -> 11
```

**S2a, S7** (as measured) **+ S5, S6, S10** (Appendix B) **+ S2c** (Appendix C) **+ S8, S9**
(Appendix F) **+ S2d, S2e, S2f** (Appendix G). **11 + 4 = 15.** The four that do **not** carry a ✅
are **S1, S2b, S3, S4**, every one of them moved out by **Appendix H** and each for the same
reason. **There is no second tally in this file; every other one is struck.**

> **⛔ THE ✅ WAS ARITHMETICALLY EARNED FAR MORE OFTEN THAN IT WAS EVIDENTIALLY EARNED, AND FOUR
> ROWS HAVE MOVED BECAUSE OF IT.** `docs/design/1.6.0-map-audit.md` (`fe8841f52`) re-derived the
> nine ✅ sources, confirmed the arithmetic of seven, and then found **the arithmetic is not the
> point**: *"three (S1, S2a, S2b) reach Δ = 0 by partitioning a set against itself rather than
> against the map."* **Appendix H re-measured each against a real denominator and found four**
> — S1, S2b, S4, and S3 twice — **and S2a reconciling against a file that has since been deleted.**
>
> §0 defines COUNT OUT as *"how many of those reached **the master list** (defined in §1) as a row
> somebody owns."* **The master list is the denominator.** `51+4+4+5+5+7 = 76`, `4+7+14 = 25`,
> `307+103+15+6+3+4+10+2+2 = 452` and Appendix D's seven buckets are all partitions of COUNT IN.
> **Any partition of *n* sums to *n*** — Δ = 0 is guaranteed before any evidence is consulted.
>
> **Of the nine, four are evidentially earned (S2a, S2c, S7, S8), one on a smaller number than it
> publishes (S9's 9 real landings, not its 21 in-document rulings), two are contested (S2b, S4) and
> two are not earned at all (S1, S3).** `S9`'s row is **left at ✅ deliberately**: its denominator
> defect is named in H.8 but it was not fully re-derived here, and moving a row on a spot check
> would be the move this appendix exists to refuse. **The structural finding is in H.9 and no
> number repairs it: three of the four master-list components are sections of `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md`,
> the fourth was deleted, and the two artefacts now holding 510 of the rows were both written
> inside the commit window S3 measures. A completeness proof needs a denominator that the work
> being measured did not create.**

> **⛔ THE DENOMINATOR WAS WRONG FROM THE FIRST COMMIT, AND THIS DOCUMENT IS THE LAST PLACE THAT
> SHOULD HAVE HAPPENED.** The table above has **fifteen** source rows, not fourteen, and it has had
> fifteen since `a0b808b79` — the first command above returns 15 against that blob too. Every tally
> in Appendices B, C and D inherited *"of 14"* rather than re-deriving it. **And the correction
> shipped broken in the same commit that made it** — at `06a9af19d` this file said, two paragraphs
> apart, both `12 of 15` (naming S8/S9 closed *"per Appendix F"*) and `Corrected here to 10 of 15 …
> 5 do not (S2d, S2e, S2f, S8, S9)`:
>
> ```sh
> git show 06a9af19d:docs/design/1.6.0-map-proof.md | grep -c '10 of 15'          # -> 4
> git show 06a9af19d:docs/design/1.6.0-map-proof.md | grep -c '12 of ~~14~~ 15'   # -> 1
> ```
>
> One commit both closed S8/S9 and listed them as open. **A tally written in more than one place is
> a tally that will disagree with itself**, which is the defect class this document exists to catch,
> found twice inside its own headline. The rule this run adopts: **one tally, one place, one
> command — the block above. Appendix lines that print a tally are struck where they stand.**

**ALL FIFTEEN SOURCES ARE WALKED, AND ⛔ `INCOMPLETE` STILL STANDS — READ WHY BEFORE READING THE
NUMBER.** S2d/S2e/S2f were recorded ⛔ `ABSENT` / `NOT LANDED`; all three exist, are tracked and are
clean, so that status was **stale, not wrong** — true at `PIN`, false since `62b58e141` /
`a5018ef67` / `45104e7f7`. **Reconciling is not finishing.** Under §0 a source reconciles when
`COUNT IN = COUNT OUT`, or when every remainder is named in prose — i.e. when **everything the
source owes has an owner**, not when it is done. The 43 rows those three sources contributed to
`1.6.0-LEDGER.md` on 2026-09-23 partition **32 `OPEN` · 4 `PARKED-OWNER` · 7 `DONE`** — derived, not
asserted:

```sh
grep -E '^\| (M5[345]|DR[0-9]+|IA[0-9]+|GS2[6-9]|GS3[0-5]) \|' docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md \
  | grep -oE '\*\*(OPEN|DONE|PARKED-OWNER)\*\*' | sort | uniq -c
# -> 7 **DONE**   32 **OPEN**   4 **PARKED-OWNER**       7 + 32 + 4 = 43
```

**Three of the four parks are money the owner must sign.** The map is now exhaustive. The work is
not finished, and
§12.3's structural finding — *there is no mechanism that turns a commit body into a map row* — is
untouched by any of this. **A file existing is not a file reconciling, and a file reconciling is not
a release shipping.**

### 4.2 S2b `1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md` — ~~Δ ≈ 30, UNRECONCILED~~ → **Δ = 0, RECONCILES**

> **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23. Read Appendix C.1 for the settled arithmetic.** The reconciliation itself
> lives in the source document, as `1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md` §6 `RECONCILIATION LEDGER (source S2b)`
> — landed at `bb83912d5`, re-verified against the working tree on 2026-09-23. **The two paragraphs
> below are the original finding and both of their numbers are wrong.** They are left in place
> because §0 requires a number that does not reproduce to be said out loud, not quietly replaced.

```sh
git show 016422be7:docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md | grep -cE '^#{2,3} '   # -> 25, NOT 33
git show 016422be7:docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md | grep -cE '^\|'        # -> 86
```

**The COUNT IN of 33 is an extraction bug in this document.** The file has 633 lines, no `####`
heading at all, and **no heading regex over the blob at PIN returns 33** — `^#{2,3} ` → 25, `^#+` →
26, `^## ` → 6, `^### ` → 19. Only two revisions of this file have ever existed, and neither yields
33. **The section count is 25.** The 86 is arithmetically right and semantically wrong: it is
`^\|` over raw markdown, which is **8 tables = 8 header + 8 separator + 70 data rows**. Sections and
rows are not disjoint populations — every row sits inside a section already counted — so no sum of
the two is an obligation count. **The unit is the §-section.**

**Settled: IN 25 · DONE 4 · OPEN 7 · NOT-AN-ITEM 14 · Δ = 0.** COUNT OUT = 3 measured *"reached the
master list"*, which is a different question from *"still owed"* — three of the four discharged
sections were discharged **in the tree**, by a gate, by the register's own data, and by the paragraph
that was the deliverable, and needed no map row. The seven genuinely open sections are enumerated in
Appendix C.1 and every one was re-checked against the tree on 2026-09-23.

~~Specifically unrepresented on the map: … and the whole of §4.2–§4.5.~~ **Withdrawn.** §4.2–§4.5 are
declared by their own parent §4 to be *"not gaps to be filled, but properties it does not measure"*.
They owe a citation prohibition, not remediation, and that prohibition is §5 — which the master list
already carries as M3.

title on line 1, and **`#![allow(dead_code)]` on line 277 — a Rust inner attribute inside a fenced
code block, counted as a heading.** The heading count is **42**. The 264 is `^\|` over raw markdown:
**24 tables = 24 header + 24 separator + 216 data rows.**

> **SUPERSEDED. The `⛔ NOT LANDED` status was true when taken and is false now.** It is kept below
> rather than deleted because §0 requires a number that no longer reproduces to be said out loud.
> **A file existing is not a file reconciling**, and the re-walk below is what separates the two.

**The original measurement, and it was correct at `PIN`:**

```sh
for f in 1.6.0-done-readout.md 1.6.0-instrument-audit.md 1.6.0-gate-sweep.md; do
  git cat-file -e 016422be7:docs/design/$f 2>/dev/null && echo "$f PRESENT" || echo "$f ABSENT"
done
# -> all three ABSENT      (still reproduces — PIN is a fixed object)
```

**All three landed AFTER `PIN`, and all three are tracked and clean at `HEAD`:**

```sh
for f in 1.6.0-done-readout.md 1.6.0-instrument-audit.md 1.6.0-gate-sweep.md; do
  git log --diff-filter=A --format="%h %ci  $f" -- docs/design/$f | tail -1
done
# -> 45104e7f7 2026-09-22 18:14:06  1.6.0-done-readout.md
# -> 62b58e141 2026-09-22 16:52:55  1.6.0-instrument-audit.md
# -> a5018ef67 2026-09-22 17:10:58  1.6.0-gate-sweep.md
git status --porcelain docs/design/1.6.0-{done-readout,instrument-audit,gate-sweep}.md   # -> (empty)
```

**"ABSENT" is not a count.** These three rows therefore take an `IN` for the first time here. Two of
the three documents (S2e, S2f) carry their own reconciliation ledger — `1.6.0-instrument-audit.md`
§10 and `1.6.0-gate-sweep.md` §12 — written against this document's §0. **Both were verified rather
than believed:** every denominator command each one prints was re-run, and every one reproduces
exactly (see below). What neither of them does is the thing §0 actually asks for — **`COUNT OUT` is
"how many reached the master list as a row somebody owns", and a document partitioning its own
contents into `DONE`/`OPEN`/`NOT-AN-ITEM` at Δ = 0 has not measured that at all.** Both self-report
`RECONCILES` on the internal partition. That claim is sound and it is not the claim §0 makes.

> **THE STRUCTURAL FINDING OF THIS SECTION, AND IT IS THE ONE THE MAP EXISTS TO CATCH: LANDING A
> DOCUMENT DID NOT TURN ITS FINDINGS INTO OWNED ROWS.** Three sources scored as holes because the
> files were missing turn out to be holes for a different and worse reason — the files are here,
> they are careful, their arithmetic reproduces, and **the work inside them still reaches no owner.**

#### 4.4a S2d `1.6.0-done-readout.md` — ~~**❌ UNRECONCILED.** 23 groups measured, 6 never asked; 6 of 14 owed items own no row~~ → **✅ RECONCILES (App. G.2).** 23 groups measured, 6 never asked; **7** of 14 owed items owned no row — all 20 now do

**COUNT IN, denominator 1 — the 23 done-oracle groups the document is a readout of.** Its own table
partitions them and **the partition reproduces exactly**:

```sh
F=docs/design/1.6.0-done-readout.md
awk '/^## THE TABLE/{f=1} /^### Reds, classified/{f=0} f' $F | grep -cE '^\| [0-9]+ \|'   # -> 23
awk '/^## THE TABLE/{f=1} /^### Reds, classified/{f=0} f' $F | grep -E '^\| [0-9]+ \|' \
  | awk -F'|' '{gsub(/^ +| +$/,"",$4); print $4}' | sort | uniq -c
# -> 10 **RED**   7 **GREEN**   6 *not measured*        7 + 10 + 6 = 23, and the headline says 7/23
```

Group numbers 1–23 are present with no gaps, and every RED and GREEN row carries a `source` column
naming where its answer came from (`umbrella` / `out-of-band` / `derived` / `prebuilt binary`).
**On its own terms this document is honest and its census is clean.**

**But 6 of the 23 have no verdict at all** — groups 2 `PLANE-PURITY`, 3 `KIND-ISOLATION`, 13
`EQUALITY`, 14 `PARITY`, 19 `KERNEL`, 21 `TELLER-STEPS`. The document says so itself and refuses to
round them away: *"A blank is not a pass… Not green, not red, not absent. **Blank.**"* — and names
the consequence: **`PARITY`, `KERNEL` and `EQUALITY`'s loop column are three of the four groups that
carry the money path.** That is a named remainder, and under §0 a named remainder is not a green.
**None of the six is on the master list as a row anybody owns.**

**COUNT IN, denominator 2 — the document's declared unit of obligation**, `## THE WORK QUEUE THIS
READOUT PRODUCES`: **item 0 plus 13 numbered rows = 14.**

```sh
awk '/^## THE WORK QUEUE/,/^## WHAT THIS DOCUMENT IS NOT/' $F | grep -cE '^\| [0-9]+ \|'   # -> 13
# + "0. Get a quiesced checkout." — the prose item the queue calls item zero          = 14
```

**COUNT OUT = ~~8~~ 7 topical, of which only 2 are attributable to this document.**

> **⚠ THE 8 IS WRONG AND THE TABLE BELOW IS THE THING THAT PROVES IT.** Seven rows carry a worklist id (`R29`, `GS21`, `GS7`, `G35`, `GS12`, `G9`, `G17`) and seven carry `—`. 7 + 7 = 14, which is the denominator; 8 + 6 = 14 also sums, **which is exactly why an off-by-one in a partition can sit undetected in a document that checks its own arithmetic**. Derived, not read:

**BOTH UNROWED RED ROWS ARE CONFIRMED LIVE RED — measured, not inferred:**

```sh
cargo build -p xtask --bin xtask && ./target/debug/xtask gate ship-ready
# -> FAIL  ship-ready:ship-twin   the ship twin is not zero
#      "…kind-isolation:legacy-drain (a transitional exemption's crate is still in the
#       tree at ship time)…"
# -> ship-ready: 4 row(s), RED
```

**AND TWO OF THE TWELVE `DONE` ROWS ARE RED AGAIN ON THE LIVE TREE.** The same run:

```sh
./target/debug/xtask gate ship-ready   # -> FAIL  ship-ready:ceiling-slack  `ceiling-slack` is not green
./target/debug/xtask gate no-deferral  # -> FAIL  no-deferral:unwaived      4 un-waived marker(s)
# crates/busbar-contract/src/signal.rs:81,:95 · busbar-oauth2/src/cimd.rs:209 · plugin-loader/src/sign.rs:86
```

§12.5 records both as `DONE`/`PASS` at `a7cceac3f`, and `1.6.0-LEDGER.md` `GS22` and `GS15` record
both as `OPEN`. **The ledger is right and the sweep is stale — but read WHY before scoring it,
because the reason inverts the finding.** The four `no-deferral` markers are in files that are
**clean at `HEAD`**; what changed is the instrument. `xtask/src/gates/no_deferral.rs` carries
**uncommitted** work whose own doc comment states the cause:

> *"**THE FOUR TAGS WERE MATCHED BY NOTHING UNTIL 2026-09-23.** The playbook has listed
> `\bTODO\b \bFIXME\b \bXXX\b \bHACK\b` under Class B since the gate was specified; the
> implementation carried the five phrases and none of the tags. The gate was therefore structurally
> incapable of saying NO about the marker class the rule is named after."*

**So S2f's `no-deferral 4/4 green` was TRUE when taken and VACUOUS the whole time** — the green
meant *"every marker I look for is waived"*, never *"there are no markers"*. That is this release's
dominant defect class landing inside a `DONE` column of a reconciliation ledger. It is **not**
S2f's error; it is the tree moving under it for the third time, exactly as §9.2 warned.

> **⚠ TWO MEASUREMENT CAVEATS, STATED RATHER THAN BURIED.** `qa/construction.toml` and
> `xtask/src/gates/{construction/rules.rs,mod.rs}` and `xtask/src/full_gate.rs` all carry
> **uncommitted** changes from a concurrent agent right now, so any `construction`-family verdict
> taken on this tree measures a ratchet nobody has committed. **`construction: hold-escapes`
> (S2f §12.5's third disputed `DONE`, `GS5`'s `OPEN`) was therefore NOT re-measured** — it cannot
> be settled honestly against a ratchet mid-edit, and the ratchet is not this document's to touch.
> Nothing in `qa/`, no golden, and no register was written to produce this section.

**Δ = 14 against `IN = 70`** (12 `DONE`-at-source + 2 live open rows unrowed), **plus 6 items
outside the 70** (3 new red rows, 3 open sections), all named above.

**S2f VERDICT: ~~⚠️ THE CLOSEST OF THE THREE, AND STILL NOT GREEN~~ → ✅ RECONCILES (App. G.4) — and re-verifying its twelve `DONE` rows against the repaired instruments cost **three** of them, one red again and two vacuous (App. G.5).** Its count reproduces, its fold
is real, 56 of 58 open rows reached an owner, and every remainder is named in the source rather
than absorbed. What holds it short: **8 items reach no owner — two of them confirmed live red by
running the gate — and its own §2 heading still disagrees with its §0 class table about whether the
withdrawn row is counted (14 vs 15).** What makes it green: row the 8, and reconcile 14 with 15.

## 5. S3 — COMMIT BODIES. THE LARGEST DELTA, AND THE MOST DANGEROUS.

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --oneline | wc -l          # -> 307
```

> Note: `--pretty=format:'%h' | wc -l` returns **306** — `--pretty=format` omits the trailing
> newline, so `wc -l` undercounts by one. The true window is **307**.

**COUNT IN: 307 commits.** The bodies in this tree are not one-liners; they are 20–60-line
argued records, and **many findings exist ONLY there** — never in a doc, never in a gate.

### 5.1 THE WINDOW WAS READ END TO END, AND IT RECONCILES

Every body was read, in two halves, each bounded by a printed command:

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --reverse --pretty=format:'===COMMIT %h%n%s%n%b' \
  | awk '/^===COMMIT/{n++} n<=154'    # half A: 154 commits, 4241fb520 … 2c9c36902
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --reverse --pretty=format:'===COMMIT %h%n%s%n%b' \
  | awk '/^===COMMIT/{n++} n>154'     # half B: 153 commits, 732844550 … a197ad2f5 (HEAD)
```

**154 + 153 = 307. The extraction reconciles against the window exactly, with no commit read twice
and none skipped.** (Verified independently: no commit body contains a `===COMMIT` line, so the
`awk` boundary cannot be fooled.)

| half | commits | residue findings |
|---|---|---|
| A (oldest 154) | 154 | **126** |
| B (newest 153) | 153 | **116** |
| | **307** ✅ | **242** |

Classified by the authors' own framing (half B's tally, which was counted; half A's distribution is
the same shape):

| class | half B | what it means |
|---|---|---|
| `OWED-WORK` | ~62 | named, unbuilt work |
| `SEPARATE-FINDING` | ~28 | a defect found and recorded rather than fixed |
| `KNOWN-RED-ON-PURPOSE` | ~20 | a gate the author states is red by design |
| `EXEMPTION-GRANTED` | 7 | a waiver/allowlist row minted in that commit |
| `OWNER-RULING` (literal string) | 4 | `2846dcff5`, `4f26fa73c`, `b65f01613`, `a197ad2f5` |
| `NONE` — fix-only, no residue | ~31 | correctly carries nothing |

**COUNT OUT: at most 45** — every master-list row, generously assuming each maps to a distinct
finding, which they do not.

**Δ ≥ 197.** Two hundred and forty-two recorded findings against a master list of at most 45 rows.

> **Eleven `OWNER RULING`-class decisions were also recorded only in bodies.** Four carry the literal
> string; eleven more are rulings under other spellings — `OWNER-LOCKED` (`50d14fd44`, `40a47a8aa`,
> `38db9d48e`, `5fbd891e0`), `OWNER-PARKED` (`5cfe127d9`), `owner-ruled`/`Ruled safe`
> (`874029471`, `14a49131d`, `295435456`, `fbead1a31`, `94cba7be6`), `open owner ruling`
> (`0209e13c0`). **A ruling recorded in eleven different spellings cannot be greped for**, which is
> the same failure mode as the banned words in §9.5.

**Δ for S3 is not an extraction artefact.** Each of the above is a sentence an author wrote *because
they could not fix it*, in the one place nothing reads back.

> **One body already found three of this document's findings.** `c6f69b6d4` — the commit that
> created the master list's M4 — states: *"Also flagged, not decided: `1.6.0-STATUS.md` and
> `1.6.0-owner-decisions-queue.md` read as current by mtime but their content is dated 09-17/18 and
> **STATUS's top recommendation is the opposite of the now-locked #19**. And the duplication census
> is cited by the spec as 'full evidence' while being **UNTRACKED**."* Two of those three are now
> fixed (`446537ad9` tracked the census). **The stale-doc one is not** (§10.1). A finding recorded
> in a body, restated in this proof four hours later, still open: that is the S3 mechanism in one
> example.

### 5.3 The structural problem this exposes

**A commit body is not a work queue.** It is append-only, unindexed, and unreadable at scale — 307
bodies in 36 hours. There is no gate that fails when a body says "owed" and no map row appears.
**That is the single highest-leverage gap this audit found**, because it is the mechanism by which
every other delta was created.

---

> **⛔ PART B WAS 2 ROWS HIGH, AND `Δ = 0` SURVIVED ON TWO ROWS THAT ARE IN NO FILE.** Exactly one
> member of the declared key families is a **dict of arrays** rather than an array —
> `rules.plane-no-money.allowlist = { "crates/busbar-llm/src/unit/verify.rs" = [ … 2 entries ] }`.
> Counting the **dict** as an array *and* its **inner array** as an array gives 27 / 131; counting
> it once gives **26 / 129**, and **the double count is precisely the 2-row gap.** Three independent
> derivations agree on 2,442: `1.6.0-ratchet-census.md` §R.1 (*"the map-proof is 2 rows high"*),
> `1.6.0-map-audit.md` D1, and the `tomllib` walker printed in App. **H.6.1**.
>
> **And Appendix D.3 finds a defect running the other way**, which stands with this one rather than
> against it: `rules.no-test-doubles-in-production.known_sites` is a TOML **table of 14 records**
> that no array walk sees — **5 `verdict = "double"`** are already bought by part C's
> `max_doubles = 5`, and **9 `verdict = "real"` are counted by no part at all.**
>
> ```
> published   2,444
>   − 2  phantom rows (B double-counts a dict of arrays)  ->  2,442   ← the count-in on the record
>   + 9  verdict="real" records no part counts (App. D.3) ->  2,451   ← the fully honest count-in
> ```
>
> **`2,444` is the one figure that is wrong in both directions at once.** *Not a money figure —
> ratchet and exception rows; no billed byte moves and no `qa/*.toml` value was written. No PARK owed.*

Deliberately **excluded** from B so the number is not padded: `patterns` and `symbols` arrays are the
rules' own needles, not exceptions to them; `teller_files` and `kernel_files.*.patterns` are scan
definitions. Including them would add 48 more and would be dishonest.

> **An extraction bug of my own, corrected.** A first pass used a line-oriented regex for the arrays
> and mis-read two multi-line ones (`hold-escapes.known_sites` as 21, `kernel-seal-impls.known_sites`
> as 9). A `tomllib` parse of the **pinned blob** gives 12 and 10. Every figure in this section is
> `tomllib` over `git show 016422be7:qa/<file>.toml`, not over the working tree — which at the time
> of writing already differs (`[[leak]]` is **724** in the working tree against **723** at `PIN`).

### 6.4 COUNT OUT = 2

Only two reach the master list: **M1 §3** names `INSTANCE-NOUN` and `KIND-ISOLATION` as two of the
16 red done-oracle groups. **Δ = 2,442.**

### 6.5 THE RECONCILIATION — and three defects it surfaced

**(a) The spec's figure for `INSTANCE-NOUN` is wrong by a factor of 27.**
`BUSBAR-1.6.0.md:1147` reads *"`INSTANCE-NOUN` (**27 rows**, burns to 0)"*. Measured:

```
rows: 724 · distinct nouns: 22 · distinct files: 412 · sum(count): 3961 · waves: 5
```

**723 rows at `PIN` (724 in the working tree), not 27.** 27 matches no measurement in that file — not rows, not nouns, not files, not
waves. Anyone planning against the spec's number is planning against nothing.

**(b) `plane-purity-strict.toml`'s own comment cites a `construction.toml` figure that is no
longer there.** It states *"(`qa/construction.toml` carries llm=565, mcp=1, a2a=3, voice=0)"*.
`construction.toml` at `PIN` carries `ports-only-tests.max_per_crate` = **0/0/0/0** for all four.
Either the debt was ratcheted to zero — in which case `plane-purity-strict`'s own `[test-reach]`
`llm = 24` is stale too — or one of the two files is lying. **Two gates measuring the same quantity
disagree, and neither is on the map.**

**(c) `contract_caps` is breached at `PIN` and the map does not say so.** `ee8e721ef`'s body:
7,426 measured against a declared 7,012, *"the gap is exactly 414, which is the `busbar-grammar`
fold's raise to `caps_contract_ceiling` (5238→5652) that was **never applied to its pair**, against
that file's own rule that the two rows are one decision measured two ways."*

**FOUR AUTHORITIES, FOUR FIGURES, for one quantity:**

| figure | source | counter |
|---|---|---|
| **5,652** | `qa/construction.toml` `gate.surface_ceilings.contract_caps` at `PIN` | the declared ceiling |
| **6,659** | `1d3d299fd` body — *"`busbar-contract` measures 6,659 against 5,652 (1,007 over)"* | `cargo xtask loc`, the new Rust counter |
| **7,012** | the *other* declared row, `rules.loc-ceilings.caps_contract_ceiling` before its raise | the pair that was never updated |
| **7,426** | `ee8e721ef` body, measured with `scripts/loc-surface.py` | **a counter that no longer exists** (§10.2) |

The two counters disagree by **767 lines** on the same crate, and the deleted one is the only
authority the spec still quotes. M4 §4 asks the owner to *"ratify `busbar-contract` at 6,659, or
drive it back to 5,652"* — **a question that omits two of the four numbers.** This is on the map as
a *question*, not as a *breach*, and the question is under-specified.

## 7. S5/S6 — the shadow-oracle registers

### 7.1 `accepted-differences.json` — 41 in, and the signature chain holds

```sh
python3 -c "import json;d=json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'));print(len(d['accepted']))"   # -> 41
```

| property | measured |
|---|---|
| entries | **41** |
| kinds | `improvement` 28 · `breaking` 10 · `additive` 3 |
| carrying a `by` (owner signature) | **41 / 41** ✅ |
| `changelog` null | **0** |
| changelog line present **verbatim** in `CHANGELOG.md` (whitespace-normalised) | **41 / 41** ✅ |

> **A correction against my own first pass.** A naive substring match reported *12 entries whose
> changelog line is absent*. That was **my extraction bug**, not a finding: 12 of the lines are
> wrapped across two lines in `CHANGELOG.md`. Normalising whitespace before comparing gives 41/41.
> Recorded here because a completeness proof that hides its own false positives is not a proof.

**Two real anomalies, neither on the map:**

1. **`D-3 money is a lookup over quantities against a dated rate-card history` carries
   `"changelog_reason": null`.** The register's own rule (its `_comment`) is: *every entry owes a
   changelog line verbatim, **or** an explicit `"changelog": null` with a `changelog_reason`*. D-3
   has a **non-null changelog and a null reason** — a field in neither legal state. `48ea84225`'s
   body independently says *"Entry D-3 is wrong … **Parked rather than fixed because it is a policy
   question** … That decides whether a published invoice can change after the fact."*
2. **`F-011r` is stale.** `48ea84225`: *"its claim that the `at` field was removed is **false**,
   `3c118f729` restored it."* The entry carries a note; it does not carry a map row.

**COUNT OUT = 1** (M4 §11, *"four shadow-oracle register rulings"*). **Δ = 40**, of which 39 are
legitimately closed by an owner signature and **2 are open anomalies** (D-3, F-011r) — D-3 is
inside M4 §11's four; **F-011r is not**.

### 7.2 `accepted-gaps.json` — 3 entries, 5 dormant, 2 with nowhere to be recorded

| id | owner | cells | what it owes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `gemini-tool-use-token-term` | llm-plane | 50 | a tool-use verb in the pinned mock **+ a product decision on the six-dialect tool-use response shape** |
| `refund-across-window` | billing | 42 | a fixture; `needs_fixture` SKIP |
| `stream-upstream-error` | llm-plane | 55 | six diagonal cells, one per streaming backend |

The file's `_comment` names **two further dormant ids that carry NO cell to name**, so they exist
only inside `accepted-differences.json`:

- **`PR2-D5`** — *"closing it needs a fallback-pool member override in `oracle-config.sh`, a new
  cell, and a **fresh 1.5.5 recording**."*
- **`S-14`** — *"a speech leaf needs a fixture, a pinned-mock speech verb, and **the product's
  refusal-envelope decision**."*

**COUNT IN = 5 dormant (3 registered + 2 documented-only). COUNT OUT = 1. Δ = 4.** Note these carry
an `owner` field but **no `by` signature field** — unlike `accepted-differences.json`, where 41/41
are signed. Under **#59** (*"every accepted-gap needs explicit owner sign-off"*) **all three
registered gaps are owed a signature they do not carry.**

## 8. S7/S8 — the done-oracle and the source markers

### 8.1 S7 `verify-1.6.0-done.sh` — 23 in, 23 out, Δ = 0. RECONCILES.

```sh
awk '/^begin_group /{n++} END{print n+0}' scripts/verify-1.6.0-done.sh    # -> 23
grep -oE 'DONE_GROUPS_DECLARED=[0-9]+' scripts/verify-1.6.0-done.sh       # -> 23
```

The script declares 23 and defines 23 — and refuses to render a verdict if they ever disagree.
Against `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` M1 §3 (*"The 16 red done-oracle groups"*):

| | groups | named |
|---|---|---|
| RED, per the spec | **16** | PLANE-DELETE, TELLER-STEPS, STORE-QA, KIND-ISOLATION, AUDIT-LEDGER, EQUALITY, DESIGN, INSTANCE-NOUN, BYTE-IDENTITY, CHANGELOG, CONFIG-STABILITY, NO-DEFERRAL, PUBLIC-HYGIENE, TEST, KERNEL, BUILD |
| not named, therefore green | **7** | PLANE-PURITY, REACHABILITY, CONFORMANCE, CONFIG-NOUN, PARITY, INVENTORY-COVERAGE, ISOMORPHISM |
| | **23** ✅ | |

**16 + 7 = 23. Δ = 0.** This is the cleanest reconciliation in the tree.

> **One naming drift:** the spec says `INSTANCE-NOUN`; the script's group is
> `INSTANCE-NOUN NEUTRALITY`. Resolvable by eye, not by a gate.
>
> **One caveat that is not small:** the spec's 16 is a *claim*, not a run. Nothing at `PIN`
> re-executes the oracle. This reconciles the **lists**; it does not verify the **verdicts**. That
> is `1.6.0-done-readout.md`'s job and it has not landed (§4.4).

### 8.2 S8 source markers — 168 raw → 51 real, and the waiver file is BROKEN

```sh
for p in TODO FIXME XXX HACK 'unimplemented!' 'todo!'; do
  git grep -c -F -- "$p" -- 'crates/**' 'xtask/**' | awk -F: '{s+=$2} END{print s+0}'
done
```

| marker | raw hits | real deferrals | reconciliation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TODO` | 77 | **2** | 75 are the config migrator's *product feature* — `migrate.rs` (32), `migrate_tests.rs` (24), `migrate_export.rs` (4) etc. **emit** `# TODO(migrate):` lines into an operator's YAML by design. The 2 real ones are `busbar-contract/src/signal.rs:81` `TODO(latency-p95)` and `:95` `TODO(outcome-signals)`. |
| `FIXME` | 0 | 0 | — |
| `XXX` | 4 | **0** | all four are the string `\uXXXX` in JSON-escaping code. False positives. |
| `HACK` | 0 | 0 | — |
| `unimplemented!` | 70 | **~49** | the `hot/` stub fixtures + doc-comment mentions |
| `todo!` | 17 | **0** | all in `xtask/src/gates/{blocking_ffi,kind_isolation,no_deferral}.rs` — **gate fixture strings**, i.e. the gates' own test corpora |
| | **168** | **51** | |

**COUNT OUT = 1** — M1 §3 names `NO-DEFERRAL` as a red group. **Δ = 50**, nominally reconciled by
`scripts/no-deferral.waivers` (52 rows, every one tagged `[retires: H5]`).

**Except the waiver file does not describe this tree.** Measured, row by row:

```
waiver rows: 52
  pointing at a live (non-comment) deferral marker: 13
  pointing at a line with NO marker (STALE):        39
real non-comment markers in those files:            51
  of which UNWAIVED:                                38
```

The `host.rs` waivers name lines **855–1217**. The actual markers are at **933–1359**. The first is
off by **+78**, the last by **+142** — and **not one of the 44 `host.rs` rows matches a marker.**

**This contradicts the record.** `1.6.0-STATUS.md:102` describes the fix as *"a mechanical **+1
line-number resync** of **44** `/hot/` rows … verified: 44 rows"*. It is not +1, it is not 44, and
it is not mechanical. The gate is red **both ways at once** — `:unwaived` (38 undeclared deferrals)
and `:stale-waiver` (39 waivers that outlived their markers), which is precisely the double-check
the gate's own header says it exists to perform.

**And the retirement is circular.** All 52 rows retire against `1.6.0-TRACKER.md:167` (H5), which
reads:

> *"52 `unimplemented!()` slots … **Out of 1.6.0 scope**; the work lands with
> `docs/design/1.6.0-protocols-as-plugins.md`."*

```sh
git cat-file -e 016422be7:docs/design/1.6.0-protocols-as-plugins.md   # -> does not exist
```

**52 deferral markers are waived, using the banned phrase, against a document that does not exist.**

## 9. THE BANNED-WORD AUDIT

**The ban (#15, `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md:141` and Part 2 row 15):** *"Banned words: defer, 1.6.x, later,
'out of scope for 1.6.0'. It is 1.6.0-or-bust."*

### 9.1 RE-MEASURED AT THE PIN — the count has moved a long way

The brief cited **11 in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` and 13 across `1.6.0-*.md`, summing to 24**. That does
not reproduce at `PIN` under any regex tried (`\bdefer(red)?\b` → 10/10; `deferr` → 9; the full
four-term set → far more). Measured, with the regex stated so the number can be argued with:

```sh
# NARROW — the ban's four terms exactly as written
python3 - <<'EOF'
import re,subprocess
PAT=re.compile(r'defer[a-zA-Z]*|1\.6\.x|\blater\b|out of scope for 1\.6\.0',re.I)
EOF
```

| scope | lines | **occurrences** |
|---|---|---|
| `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` | 29 | **39** |
| `docs/design/1.6.0-*.md` (36 files) | 49 | **54** |
| **TOTAL** | **78** | **93** |

> **The narrow regex under-counts, and the miss is the important one.** The ban is written as
> *"out of scope for 1.6.0"*. `1.6.0-TRACKER.md:167` says **"Out of 1.6.0 scope"** — the same
> phrase, reordered, and the narrow regex sails straight past it. Widening to `out of .{0,8}scope`
> adds 6 lines / 9 occurrences in the siblings (**63**), for a wide total of **102**.
>
> **A ban enforced by a literal-string grep is a ban that rewards rephrasing.** This is a finding in
> its own right and has no map row.

### 9.2 THE MEASUREMENT MOVED MID-AUDIT

Measured on the working tree at the start: **49 lines / 54 occurrences across 36 sibling files.**
Re-measured on the working tree 40 minutes later: **43 lines / 44 occurrences across 34 files** —
because another agent deleted `1.6.0-STATUS.md` and `1.6.0-owner-decisions-queue.md` from the
working tree while this audit was running. Both are **still in the commit tree at `PIN`**; the
deletion is staged in the shared index and uncommitted. All numbers here read from `git show`.

### 9.3 THE CLASSIFICATION — 93 occurrences, three buckets

| bucket | `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` | `1.6.0-*.md` | **TOTAL** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **(a) a quotation of the ban itself, or the gate that enforces it** | 15 | 7 | **22** |
| **(b) innocuous prose** — English "afterwards", a filename, a product term, or an argument *against* deferring | 22 | 28 | **50** |
| **(c) A REAL DEFERRAL HIDING IN WORDS** | **2** | **19** | **21** |
| | **39** | **54** | **93** ✅ |

**22 + 50 + 21 = 93.** The three buckets sum to the measured total.

### 9.4 EVERY (c) — each one is a list item and a finding

**In `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` (2):**

| line | the words | what is actually being deferred |
|---|---|---|
| `:693`/`:699` | *"METERING … (reserve/settle is an **EXTENSION POINT, not 1.6.0**)"* · *"`CostHold` is DEMOTED … for the future **high-rate-carrier minor bump** — a reserved shape, not implemented wiring"* | `cost_reserve`/`cost_settle` wiring. *"not 1.6.0"* is "out of scope for 1.6.0" with the words changed, and *"minor bump"* is 1.6.x with the words changed. **`1.6.0-duplex-plane-and-realtime.md` mandates mid-session `cost_reserve`/`cost_settle` — the two documents are in direct conflict and no row owns it.** |
| `:3073` | *"`keys/verb/rate/restart/posture/…` (4.7k) = verb-execution semantics, **still owed a home**; the crate split is **a later wave**."* | 4,700 lines with no destination, assigned to a wave that is not in Part 5's W0–W8. |

**In the sibling docs (19):**

| file:line | the words | what is deferred |
|---|---|---|
| `1.6.0-exact-count-migration.md:1` | **title:** *"The exact-count conversion — **what a later wave must do**"* · *"Status: a NOTE, not a migration. **Nothing in this document has been performed.**"* | 350 lines of #81-mandated call-site conversion. **#81 is OWNER-LOCKED 2026-09-22.** |
| `1.6.0-TRACKER.md:167` | *"**Out of 1.6.0 scope**; the work lands with `1.6.0-protocols-as-plugins.md`"* | 52 `unimplemented!()` ABI slots, against a **nonexistent document** (§8.2) |
| `1.6.0-TRACKER.md:105` ×2 | *"CG-49 **deferred**"* · *"(49 **deferred**)"* | contract gap 49 |
| `1.6.0-contract-gaps.md:236` | *"SNI (**defer**)."* | multi-cert SNI |
| `1.6.0-composition-root-plan.md:331`, `:794` | *"CG-49 **defers** multi-cert SNI, **correctly**, because PB-101 makes a single-cert listener parity-complete"* | …but the same table's row 8 says *"`tls::accept` hardcodes slot 0, which **breaks the single-cert path**"* — **the justification for the deferral is void** |
| `1.6.0-duplex-plane-and-realtime.md:735` | *"**II.5 Scope: 1.6.0 vs later**"* · *"the realtime/duplex plane is a **1.7.0 plane, NOT a 1.6.0 deliverable**"* | contradicts locked **#45** (streaming/voice billable cut in 1.6.0) and **#18** (voice is a streaming dialect) |
| `1.6.0-duplex-plane-and-realtime.md:517`, `:519` | *"Scope of property #3 **now vs later**"* · *"**defer** the rest to **Phase 5**?"* | the session/handle engine — an open question phrased in banned words |
| `1.6.0-TRACKER.md:98` | *"busbar-transport-*: http, sse, ws, grpc, stdio, tcp, tls (in-tree; **peer later with fleet**)"* | **#39** requires every plugin be 1 repo / 1 crate with no plugin source in `busbar`; seven transports stay in-tree |
| `1.6.0-duplication-census.md:823` | *"a doc comment **acknowledging the debt and deferring it to decision #81**"* | a banned word **in shipped source**, not just a doc |
| `1.6.0-secret-hygiene.md:236` | *"The doc's recommendation is to **defer** `Held` unless a concrete audit call site needs it"* | the `Held` secret carrier (**#53/#54** are OWNER-LOCKED) |
| `1.6.0-STATUS.md:109` | *"**This is NOT banned 'defer'** — core deletion isn't a 1.6.0 feature, it's cleanup"* | **the opposite of locked #19.** A deferral arguing it is not one. |
| `1.6.0-STATUS.md:101` | records that H5 uses *"Out of 1.6.0 scope"* — *"a DECISIONS #15 banned phrase"* | the finding was made 4 days ago and the phrase is still there |
| `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md:61` | decision 13's **Your ask** column reads *"yes / no / **later**"* | the banned word offered to the owner **as a menu option** |
| `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md:591` | *"**C — Defer export-as-plugin past 1.6.0** and strike the four roster slots"* | same — a banned option presented for selection |
| `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md:792` | *"**C — Later.** Cost: every wave above adds a constant to the collapse."* | same |
| `1.6.0-security-posture.md:457` | *"bug bounty programme (safe-harbor first, **funded later**)"* | a programme commitment with no date |
| `1.6.0-wave-d-work-order.md:97` | *"**UNMAPPED** — plan §4.1 assigns no destination, or the assignment is **explicitly deferred/undecided**"* | **2,702 lines** with no destination, plus 4 named wave-D blockers with *"no destination that already exists"* |

**Of the 21 (c) items, 2 have a master-list row** (M4 §8 touches the export-as-plugin option; M2
item 5 is adjacent to the `PlaneDeclaration`/SNI cluster). **19 do not.**

### 9.5 The pattern worth naming

Not one of the 21 says "deferred". They say *extension point*, *a later wave*, *a 1.7.0 plane*,
*Phase 5*, *peer later with fleet*, *minor bump*, *still owed a home*, *a NOTE not a migration*, and
— twice — they say *this is not a deferral*. **The ban changed the vocabulary, not the behaviour.**
A grep-enforced ban on four literal strings cannot see any of this, which is why it has to be read.

## 10. STALE DOCS THAT READ AS CURRENT, AND CITED DOCS THAT DO NOT EXIST

### 10.1 The two stale documents — CONFIRMED, and the fix has NOT landed

| doc | mtime | content dated | status at `PIN` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `docs/design/1.6.0-STATUS.md` | current | **2026-09-17/18** | **STILL IN THE COMMIT TREE** |
| `docs/design/1.6.0-owner-decisions-queue.md` | current | **2026-09-17/18** | **STILL IN THE COMMIT TREE** |

**The reported contradiction is real.** `1.6.0-STATUS.md`'s top-ranked queued item is:

> *"**⚠ TOP DECISION — is busbar-core deletion IN 1.6.0?** … PLAN says busbar-core deletion is NOT
> a cut blocker … Under **B** (core deletion = post-1.6.0 arc) 1.6.0 ships on the byte-identical
> engine"*

Locked **#19** (`BUSBAR-1.6.0.md:336`) is the exact opposite:

> *"**busbar-core and busbar-voice are DELETED in 1.6.0 — the deletion IS the release.**"*

And `1.6.0-STATUS.md:109` argues the position is legal: *"This is NOT banned 'defer'."*

**Two further measured defects in the same file:** its item 6 (*"mechanical +1 line-number resync of
44 rows"*) is wrong in all three particulars (§8.2), and its item 4 (*"Integrate + push … your
8am"*) instructs an action against a tip from 2026-09-17.

`1.6.0-owner-decisions-queue.md` is superseded in the same way: its *"3 gates only YOU can clear"*
are the money-oracle `SUPERVISOR_MARKERS` block and a 2,301-commit `dev` reconciliation against a
feature line (`busbar-1.6.0-config-neutral-half-parse-byte-safe`) that is not the current branch.

**Both violate #58** (*one canonical doc per topic, delete-don't-supersede*) **and #14** (*no stale
line may survive for an agent to resurrect*).

> ### THE FIX IS ASSERTED BUT NOT LANDED — and that is worse than either state
>
> `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md` already tells the owner, in a block quote, that this was handled:
>
> > *"**Two stale documents were DELETED rather than left to mislead you.** … both files are
> > **GONE**."*
>
> At `PIN`, `git ls-tree 016422be7 docs/design/` **still lists both**. The deletion exists only as a
> staged `D` in the shared index, uncommitted. So the tree now carries a document asserting that two
> misleading documents are gone, while they are not — which is a third misleading document.
>
> **RECOMMENDATION: commit the deletion.** Not a pointer stub — #58 forbids that, and
> `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md` already carries forward the one thing in STATUS worth keeping (the
> streaming-metering recommendation, absorbed into its §18.6). Until the deletion is a commit, the
> assertion should be struck from `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md`.

the spec (the table above), and **2 documents that DO exist should not** (§10.1).

> be deleted under #80, and `692ef8afd`/`524cfc58a` deleted it. Not a finding; listed so the
> extraction's own exclusions are visible.

## 11. WHAT THIS PROOF DID *NOT* COVER

Named so the gaps in the *proof* are as visible as the gaps in the *map*:

1. **The S3 delta is a floor, not a ceiling.** All 307 bodies were read and the halves reconcile
   (§5.1), but "242 residue findings" is a count of *statements*, not of *distinct items*: several
   are the same defect recorded by two commits (the `contract_caps` pair appears in both
   `ee8e721ef` and `a9a71b961`; the two-admin-reads money divergence appears in three). A
   de-duplicated count would be smaller — and still an order of magnitude above 45.
2. **The 36-hour window is itself an arbitrary boundary.** Work owed before 2026-09-21 05:00 and
   recorded only in a body is invisible to this audit.
3. ~~**No gate was executed.**~~ **SUPERSEDED — this is no longer true and the correction matters,
   because three separate findings turned on running a gate rather than reading it.** §6/§8's
   verdicts were read from configuration and source; Appendix D ran three gates, Appendix F ran the
   `no-deferral` pair against a planted marker, and **Appendix G ran ten** — `no-deferral`,
   `no-deferral-strict-done`, `qa-names`, `structure-lint`, `ship-ready`, `construction`,
   `instance-noun-neutrality`, `audit-ledger`, `config-schema` (gate and selftest) and
   `design-bindings --selftest` — **ten gates**, plus `xtask ledger --check`. **Reading instead of
   running would have got three of them wrong:** `construction`'s
   thirteen *"DID NOT RUN"* rows now run (§G.4.2), `hold-escapes` is PASS with its ratchet untouched
   (§G.4.3), and `ship-ready:ceiling-slack` is FAIL where the record says PASS (§G.5.2).
4. ~~**The 16 claimed red done-oracle groups were not re-run** (§8.1).~~ **PARTLY ANSWERED, and the
   answer was worse than the question.** `1.6.0-done-readout.md` is a real run of all 23 groups and
   it partitions **7 GREEN / 10 RED / 6 that returned no verdict at all** — so the figure in
   circulation was never 16 reds, and six groups, three of them on the money path, were never asked
   (§G.2, `M53`–`M55`, `DR14`–`DR16`).
5. **`qa/kind-isolation.toml`'s 205 `[[cell]]` / 194 `[[dep]]` / 118 `[[edge]]` rows were counted,
   not adjudicated.** Some are records, not debt. The 13 `[[disagreement]]`, 6 `[[question]]` and 5
   `[[transitional]]` rows are unambiguously owed work and none is on the map.
6. **The 21 items of `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md` were not mapped 1:1 onto the spec's 15 open M2
   items.** The two lists use different numbering and overlap only partially; a true cross-walk is
   itself a piece of work and is recommended below.
7. **Plugin repositories were not examined.** #31/#39 put every plugin in its own repo; nothing
   outside `GetBusbar/busbar` was read.

## 12. VERDICT

# ⛔ INCOMPLETE

**The claim "everything is mapped" cannot be defended at `016422be7`.** Three of ~~fourteen~~ **fifteen**
sources reconcile. The rest do not, and the failures are not rounding.

> **~~TALLY AS OF 2026-09-23 — `10 of 15`~~ — STRUCK. THE TALLY LIVES IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE AND THIS IS
> NOT IT: §2, *THE HONEST TALLY*, which derives it by command.** The *"three"* above is the
> as-measured figure at `PIN` and is kept for that reason. All fifteen sources now reconcile
> (Appendix B: S5/S6/S10 · C: S2b/S2c · D: S4 · E: S3 · F: S8/S9 · G: S2d/S2e/S2f).
> **`⛔ INCOMPLETE` STILL STANDS, AND THE REASON HAS CHANGED.** It is no longer *"sources nobody can
> account for"*; it is §12.3 — nothing turns a commit body into a map row, so the map is exhaustive
> today and has a half-life measured in hours. **A reconciled source is one whose every remainder has
> an owner. It is not one whose work is done.**

| **S7** — the 23 done-oracle groups | **Δ = 0.** 16 red + 7 green = 23, exactly. |

### 12.3 The one structural finding, if only one survives

**There is no mechanism that turns a commit body into a map row.** 307 commits in 36 hours, 242
recorded residue findings, bodies carrying ship blockers, live breaches and instrument defects — and
nothing reads them back. Every other delta in this table was manufactured by that gap.

Sources **S1, S2a and S7 reconcile precisely because each has a counter that refuses**:
`DONE_GROUPS_DECLARED` fails the run the moment it stops matching `begin_group`; the marker sweep is
a fixed regex over a fixed file; the decision list is `^## N.`-numbered. **Every source with a
refusing counter reconciled. Every source without one did not.** That is the whole result, and it
predicts the fix.

The corollary the tree already wrote down, in `eb36d1695`: *"a gate that cannot be shown failing is
not evidence."* The same applies to a map: **a map with no counter that can go red is not a map, it
is a list.**

### 12.4 What would move this to COMPLETE

Ordered by delta closed per unit of effort:

1. ~~**Land the three absent documents** (§4.4).~~ **DONE, and it was not enough** — all three landed
   (`45104e7f7` / `62b58e141` / `a5018ef67`) and **landing them did not turn one finding into an
   owned row.** That took Appendix G and 43 ledger rows. **Replace this item with the lesson:
   a source is not folded when its file exists; it is folded when its last item has an owner.**
2. **De-duplicate the 242 S3 findings into owned rows.** This is the largest delta and the one that
   regenerates the others. The raw extraction exists; what is missing is adjudication.
3. **Commit the deletion of the two stale docs** — and, until then, strike the claim in
   `1.6.0-owner-decisions.md` that they are *"GONE"* (§10.1).
4. **Repair `scripts/no-deferral.waivers`** by regeneration, not by a `+1` — and give H5 a real
   retirement condition, since `1.6.0-protocols-as-plugins.md` does not exist (§8.2).
5. **Fix the six dangling authority citations** (`:314`, `:331`, `:333`, `:755`, `:791`, `:884`) and
   strike the settled `PARKED` at `:2585` (§3.1, §10.2).
6. **Reconcile the four `contract_caps` numbers** (5,652 / 6,659 / 7,012 / 7,426) — which means
   first deciding which counter is authoritative now that `scripts/loc-surface.py` is deleted — and
   correct `INSTANCE-NOUN` from 27 to 724 (§6.5).
7. **Rule the 21 (c) deferrals** — in or out, in words that are not banned (§9.4).
8. **Cross-walk M2's 15 open items against M4's 21** and merge to one numbering (§11.6), striking
   M2 item 4 (already ruled), collapsing M2 items 9/14 (duplicates) and M4 item 1 (already ruled) —
   §1.1.
9. **Add the refusing counter.** A commit body containing *owed* / *still owes* / *separate
   finding* / *parked* / *left red* that lands no map row should fail CI, the way
   `DONE_GROUPS_DECLARED` already fails the done-oracle. **This is the only item on this list that
   stops the deltas regrowing.** Without it, this document is a snapshot with a 36-hour half-life.

# APPENDIX A — THE 21 ADJUDICATED, THE GATE REBUILT, THE DETECTOR WIDENED

> **Appended 2026-09-22.** §§0–12 above are another agent's work and are not edited here. This
> appendix answers the three things §9 left open: it **rules on every (c) item**, it **rebuilds
> `scripts/no-deferral.waivers` against the real tree**, and it **replaces the literal-string grep
> with a shape detector** — then reports, honestly, the detector's untuned miss rate.
>
> Everything below is measured on the **working tree at 2026-09-22**, not at `PIN`, because the
> question "is this still owed?" can only be asked of the tree that will ship. Where a `PIN` line
> number and a working-tree line number differ, both are given. Every claim carries the command.

## A.0 THE HEADLINE, BEFORE THE DETAIL

**Of the 21, four were real, open, unowned 1.6.0 work. Thirteen were STALE — the work had already
landed and only the words survived. Four were correctly not-1.6.0 and needed rewriting, not doing.**

That ratio is the finding, and it cuts both ways. **It is good news** that thirteen "deferrals" were
finished work whose prose never caught up. **It is bad news for exactly the same reason:** a tree
where two thirds of the deferral language is stale is a tree where nobody can tell a real deferral
from a fossil by reading — which is how four genuine ones stayed invisible, and why one of them had
been suppressed by a broken waiver file for long enough that the record described the breakage as "a
mechanical +1 resync".

**And a correction against this appendix's own first draft, stated here rather than buried:** the
first pass ruled the `cost_reserve`/`cost_settle` metering slots OPEN, on the reasoning that the
plane side was ported while the host side was still `unimplemented!()`. **That was wrong.** The
`unimplemented!()` bodies in `crates/busbar-plugin/src/hot/host.rs` are `mod stub`, a compile-surface
fixture; the real host is 224 lines at `crates/busbar-kernel/src/plane_host/cost_host.rs`, wired at
`vtable.rs:121-122`. The slots shipped. The error is recorded because it is the *same* error the
waiver file institutionalised — reading a fixture as the thing it is a fixture for — and an appendix
about misread markers that quietly fixed its own misread marker would be worth less than one that
says where it slipped.

## A.1 THE ARITHMETIC, INCLUDING ONE THE SOURCE DOES NOT RECONCILE

§9.3's bucket table gives bucket (c) as **2 (in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md`) + 19 (siblings) = 21**. §9.4 then
cites **three** lines in `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` (`:693`, `:699`, `:3073`) and **19** occurrences across 17
sibling rows. **2 ≠ 3.** The source does not reconcile its own bucket count against its own citation
list, and the gap is exactly the kind of +1 that lets one item vanish.

**This appendix adjudicates all 22 distinct lines**, so the discrepancy cannot hide anything
whichever number is right. Occurrence arithmetic: 22 lines carry 23 occurrences (`1.6.0-TRACKER.md`
`:105` holds the word twice), against §9.3's 21. Ruling on 22 and saying so is the only honest way to
close a count that does not close.

## A.3 WHY H5's FIXTURES ARE OUTCOME (2) AND NOT A DODGE

This is the one ruling that most resembles the evasion this exercise exists to catch, so it gets its
proof rather than its assertion.

`mod stub` / `PlaneHostVtable::STUB` / `PlaneDecl::STUB` are a **compile-surface proof**: each fn
carries the exact fn-pointer signature of its vtable slot, so the module type-checks that every
`extern "C-unwind"` slot has a real, well-typed signature, and a STUB slot panics *because nothing is
meant to dispatch through it*. The test that it is a fixture and not a postponement is **who calls
it**, and that is measurable:

```sh
grep -rn 'PlaneHostVtable::STUB\|PlaneDecl::STUB' crates/ | grep -v '^crates/busbar-plugin/src/hot/\(host\|decl\).rs:'
# -> crates/busbar-plugin/src/hot/tests/host_tests.rs:77,128,138,145,157,222
#    crates/busbar-plugin/src/hot/tests/decl_tests.rs:29
```

**Seven call sites, all of them the tests that check its types.** A fixture whose only callers are
its own type-checks is finished, and no future release will "fix" it — it gets deleted when the thing
it stands in for arrives.

**What the H5 wording was hiding is a different row, and that one IS 1.6.0.** `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md`
§11a makes *"`PlaneHost` (+ cost carrier) has REAL production riders (dogfooded, step 2) — no
'0-caller ABI'"* a **CUT GATE** (§11, *"rerun … as CUT GATES … and GATE the 1.6.0 cut"*). And the gap
is **not** the one the phrase suggests:

```sh
sed -n '36,140p' crates/busbar-kernel/src/plane_host/vtable.rs | grep -cE '^\s+[a-z_]+: Some\('
# -> 44          (44 of 44 slots wired to real impls; 13,054 lines under plane_host/)
grep -rln 'PlaneHostVtable' crates/busbar-plane-* crates/busbar-{llm,mcp,a2a,voice} --include='*.rs'
# -> (empty)     ZERO plane crates cross the C-ABI vtable
```

**The host is built; nothing dogfoods it.** All four installed planes ride the neutral Rust seam
(`EngineHost`/`MeteringHost`); the C-ABI vtable is crossed only host-to-itself inside the kernel, and
`plane_host/mod.rs:190` says so: *"ADDITIVE and currently unridden: no plane dispatch is wired yet."*
That is **H6**, and **nothing in CI reds on it** — `plane-abi-neutrality`'s six rows are neutrality
and presence checks, not rider counts, so §11a is today a prose gate adjudicated by a human rerun.

**The two honest outcomes are: land a rider, or have the owner amend §11a's bullet.** "Ship and call
§11a satisfied" is not one of them.

## A.4 PART B — THE WAIVER FILE, REBUILT AGAINST THE TREE

### A.4.1 The measurement reproduced, from the gate itself

§8.2's numbers are exactly right, and the gate says so unprompted:

```
$ cargo xtask gate no-deferral
FAIL no-deferral:unwaived       38 un-waived marker(s): …host.rs:1005 … host.rs:1359
FAIL no-deferral:stale-waiver   38 stale waiver(s):     …host.rs:855  … host.rs:1217
```

**Red in both directions at once** — 38 real markers nobody waived, and 38 waivers pointing at lines
holding nothing. Not one of the 44 `host.rs` rows matched a marker. The record called this *"a
mechanical **+1** line-number resync of **44** rows … verified"*; the drift is **+142** at both ends,
`decl.rs` did not move at all, and a "+1 resync" cannot be mechanical when one of the two files is
stationary.

### A.4.2 The marker census, taken the only way that can be trusted

Line numbers for the rebuild were **not read off the file by eye** — that is what produced the
revision being replaced. They came out of the gate, by emptying the row list so every marker reports
as un-waived:

```sh
cp scripts/no-deferral.waivers /tmp/w.bak
grep '^#' scripts/no-deferral.waivers > /tmp/hdr && cp /tmp/hdr scripts/no-deferral.waivers
cargo xtask gate no-deferral        # :unwaived now enumerates EVERY real marker, in order
cp /tmp/w.bak scripts/no-deferral.waivers
# -> 52 un-waived marker(s): decl.rs:227,239,246,250,254,263,272,279
#                            host.rs:997,1001,…,1359   (44 slots, in vtable order)
```

**52 markers: 8 in `decl.rs`, 44 in `host.rs`.** Every row of the rebuilt file is one of these, with
its slot name read from the marker's own `unimplemented!("…")` string. The procedure is written into
the file's header so the next line-drift is a *verified* resync rather than a claimed one.

### A.4.3 The retirement condition — the half that was worse, and quieter

All 52 rows retired against H5, which retired against
`docs/design/1.6.0-protocols-as-plugins.md` — **a file that has never existed in this repository**
(and is cited four more times in `1.6.0-duplex-plane-and-realtime.md`).

**That is the more dangerous half of the defect, not the lesser one.** The 39 bad anchors were loud:
the gate named them every single run. **An unreachable exit condition is silent.** A waiver whose
retirement test cannot be evaluated never retires — it is permanent *by accident* rather than by
decision, and while it happens it looks identical to a healthy row.

Both conditions are now **commands that run against this tree**, naming no document:

```sh
grep -rn 'PlaneHostVtable::STUB\|PlaneDecl::STUB' crates/   # empty      => H5 retires
grep -rln 'PlaneHostVtable' crates/busbar-plane-*           # non-empty  => H6 retires
```

### A.4.4 Green — and proven green for the right reason

```
$ cargo xtask gate no-deferral-strict-done
PASS no-deferral:waiver-shape · discovery-floor · unwaived · stale-waiver · strict-done   (5 rows, green)
```

Every one of the 52 real markers is now waived **with a reason and an owner**; every waiver names a
live line. **Nothing was silenced to get there** — the row count did not change, because the marker
count did not change. What changed is that the rows now describe the tree.

**Red-before-green, so the green means something.** Moving a single row by one line:

```
$ sed -i '' 's|host.rs:1348\t|host.rs:1347\t|' scripts/no-deferral.waivers && cargo xtask gate no-deferral
FAIL no-deferral:unwaived        FAIL no-deferral:stale-waiver
```

One line of drift in one of 52 rows reds the gate in both directions. That is the check the old file
was structurally incapable of failing usefully, because it was already failing everywhere.

## A.5 PART C — THE DETECTOR, AND WHAT IT STILL CANNOT DO

`scripts/deferral-words.py`. Two layers, and the difference is the point.

**Layer 1 — named shapes, the enforcement set.** 22 regex families, case-insensitive, each derived
from a phrasing this tree actually used: `defer-family`, `out-of-scope`, `next-version`,
`extension-point`, `phase-n`, `wave-later`, `future-release`, `not-yet-done`, `negated-now`,
`undecided`, `for-now`, `partial-coverage`, `approximation`, `stub-admission`, `someone-elses-row`,
`denies-deferral`, and others.

**Layer 2 — two structural rules, the reading list.** No deferral vocabulary at all. **(A)** a
future-time signal paired with a work signal in one line; **(B)** a modal obligation paired with an
indefinite or absent agent. Noisy by construction, never a gate — its job is the phrasing nobody
enumerated.

### A.5.1 It names every one of the 21 — including the one the ban's own grep could not see

```sh
git archive 016422be7 docs/design | tar -x -C /tmp/pin-tree   # measure at PIN, as §9 did
python3 scripts/deferral-words.py --expect /tmp/the21.txt
# -> 22/22 named, 0 MISSED
```

| the ban's four literals (§9.1's regex) | the shape detector |
|---|---|
| `1.6.0-TRACKER.md:167` *"Out of 1.6.0 scope"* → **MISS** | → **`out-of-scope`** |

That single miss is what waived 52 ABI slots. Widening measured: over the same 1.6.0 doc set, Layer 1
reports **665 occurrences on 575 lines across 40 files**, against the narrow regex's 93 — roughly
seven times the surface, and the 21 are all inside it.

### A.5.2 THE POSITIVE CONTROL, PLANTED IN A REAL FILE — BOTH HALVES REPORTED

**Control A — the ban, rephrased.** Three comments planted in
`crates/busbar-plugin/src/hot/decl.rs`, then reverted:

```
crates/…/decl.rs:6  out-of-scope     // …multi-cert SNI sits outside this release's scope; the slot stays reserved.
crates/…/decl.rs:7  extension-point  // …this is an extension point, not something 1.6.0 ships wiring for.
crates/…/decl.rs:8  next-version     // …the remaining slots get real hosts in the 1.7.0 plane work.
old narrow grep on the same three lines: MISS · MISS · MISS   (0/3)
```

**3/3 new, 0/3 old.** The widening does the job it was built for.

**Control B — five phrasings I invented *after* the shape set was frozen, planted the same way, and
never used to tune anything. This is the honest test, and it mostly FAILED:**

```
** MISSED BY BOTH LAYERS **  // The window accounting rounds to the minute; per-second is more plumbing than this ticket bought.
L2 future:when+work:prove    // One provider is enough to prove the seam. The other two land when someone needs them.
** MISSED BY BOTH LAYERS **  // This assumes the config never reloads. It does, but not on any path we ship.
** MISSED BY BOTH LAYERS **  // I have left the old branch in place because deleting it needs a migration nobody has written.
** MISSED BY BOTH LAYERS **  // Enough of the contract to compile. The invariants are documented and unenforced.
-> 1/5
```

**1 of 5. I am reporting that rather than fixing it, and the refusal is deliberate.** Two earlier
rounds *were* used to widen the shape set — disclosed in the file, and both widenings were new
SHAPE CLASSES (`negated-now`; `partial-coverage`; `approximation`; `stub-admission`;
`someone-elses-row`; Layer 2's obligation rule), never the words of the phrase that exposed them.
Round 3 is what the detector does on prose it has not seen. **1 in 5.**

So, plainly: **Layer 1 is a shape set, not a complete one, and no amount of widening will close
this.** "More plumbing than this ticket bought" and "documented and unenforced" are deferrals with no
deferral vocabulary, no future tense, and no unowned modal. A text detector cannot reach them, and
tuning it against each new example just rebuilds the spelling list one word at a time — which is the
exact failure the four-literal grep already demonstrated.

**The correct conclusion is the one §9.5 reached and this appendix confirms with a number: the ban
cannot be enforced by a detector alone.** The detector's real job is to make the 665 candidate lines
*findable* so a human can read them, and to make the 93→665 widening permanent so the cheapest
evasion — reordering two words — stops working. The reading is still the gate. `--layer=2` and
`--summary` exist for exactly that, and `--selftest` prints the 1-in-5 every time it is run, so the
limitation is stated by the tool rather than remembered about it.

Seven are engineering (1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 — two of which, 3 and 4, are the ones that were genuinely

the tree does not meet with no CI row to say so, and **8,758 lines** of OWNER-LOCKED #39 work with no
owner of any kind — sitting behind a ticked `[x]` and the words *"peer later with fleet"*.

- *`#86` cited at `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md:1572` and non-existent (the hook freeze is `#85`)* — real, and

to prevent.

# APPENDIX B — S5, S6 AND S10 RECONCILED (2026-09-22, post-PIN re-walk)

> **What this appendix is.** §2 left S5 ⚠️ (*"reconciled as signed; 2 anomalies"*), S6 ⚠️
> (*"partially reconciled"*) and S10 ❌ (*"UNRECONCILED"*). This appendix closes all three to the
> §0 standard — **count-in = count-out, or every non-zero remainder explained in prose.**
>
> **Measured on the WORKING TREE at 2026-09-22 (`consolidated/1.6.0`), not at `PIN`.** That is
> deliberate and it is the point: S10 in particular has moved a long way since `016422be7`, and a
> reconciliation that reports a stale count is not a reconciliation. Every number below carries the
> command that produces it against the tree as it stands.
>
> **Nothing in `accepted-differences.json` or `accepted-gaps.json` was read-modified.** Both are
> money-adjacent release evidence and the oracle is never waived (#10). Every defect found in them
> below is a **PARK** — entry, root cause, recommendation — for the owner. No entry was edited,
> re-signed, blessed or deleted.
