## B.1 S5 — `accepted-differences.json`. **RECONCILES. Δ = 0.**

### B.1.1 The count, and the buckets that absorb it

```sh
python3 -c "import json;d=json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'));print(len(d['accepted']))"   # -> 41
```

**COUNT IN = 41.** Every entry was put to the four questions the reconciliation owes — *is it still
needed · is its signature valid · does the difference it accepts still occur · is it attributable to
a decision* — and every one lands in exactly one bucket:

| bucket | n | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **A — LIVE AND TRUE** | **11** | premise verified against code, the `v1.5.5` tag, the goldens and the pinned mock; subject live; signature covers what the entry does |
| **B — LIVE, TRUE, UNDER-DESCRIBED** | **12** | the premise holds but does not describe the whole of what the entry forgives; the acceptance is real, the *reason* is narrower than the *grant* |
| **C — PREMISE FALSIFIED (the difference no longer occurs, or never did)** | **14** | STALE-BY-FIX or DEV-ONLY-BASELINE or WRONG-CELL; **dead weight that still reads as an approval** |
| **D — NO LIVE SUBJECT AT ALL (matches zero owed cells)** | **4** | the regex reaches nothing the oracle runs; **green over nothing** |
| | **41** | |
| **COUNT OUT (rows on the master list)** | **1** | M4 §11, *"four shadow-oracle register rulings"* |
| **Δ** | **40** | |

**Δ = 40, and all 40 are explained, not counted.** 39 carry an owner signature that closes them as a
decision; the 40th is `F-011r`, which is signed but whose signed premise is false, and which M4 §11's
four do **not** include. Under §0's rule — *"a source RECONCILES when Δ is zero or every non-zero
remainder is explained in prose"* — **S5 RECONCILES.** The register is not a hole in the map; it is a
signed ledger with 4 dead rows and 14 false reasons, and those are now named.

The per-entry adjudication is `docs/design/1.6.0-register-audit.md` (`1a4c66799`, 884 lines, entry by
entry, read-only). Its three headline counts — 11 TRUE / 12 TRUE-but-under-described / 18 FALSIFIED —
and its scope arithmetic were **re-derived independently here and reproduce exactly**:

```sh
# every regex against the corpus and the golden ledger
python3 - <<'PY'
import json,re
a=json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'))['accepted']
cells=[c['id'] for c in json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/cells.json'))['cells']]
led=dict(l.split('\t')[:2] for l in open('testing/shadow-oracle/golden/1.5.5/ledger.tsv'))
bad=[e['id'] for e in a if len([c for c in cells if re.search(e['cells'],c)])!=e['expected_cells']]
owed=set()
for e in a: owed|={c for c in cells if re.search(e['cells'],c) and led.get(c)=='PASS'}
print('width mismatches:',bad)                 # -> []   all 41 exact
print('owed cells pre-authorised:',len(owed))  # -> 545  of 916 PASS
PY
```

- **2,318 cells · 916 `PASS` (owed) · 1,402 `SKIP`.**
- **All 41 `expected_cells` are exact.** The *scope* axis of this register is healthy.
- **545 of the 916 owed cells carry a pre-authorised change — 59.5%.** Reproduced independently of
  the spec sentence that first claimed it.

### B.1.2 THE TWO ANOMALIES §7.1 WARNED ABOUT, STATED EXPLICITLY

§7.1 said *"2 anomalies"* and left them as a sentence. **A warning nobody has read is not a
reconciliation.** Here they are, named, with the command that finds each and the exact remedy.

#### ANOMALY 1 — `D-3` is in a field state the register's own rule forbids

```sh
python3 -c "
import json
for e in json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'))['accepted']:
    if 'changelog_reason' in e: print(e['id'].split()[0], '| changelog:', e['changelog'] is not None, '| reason:', e['changelog_reason'])
"
# -> F-011c | changelog: True  | reason: <a real sentence>
# -> D-3    | changelog: True  | reason: None          <<< ILLEGAL
```

Exactly **two** of the 41 carry a `changelog_reason` key at all. `F-011c`'s is a real sentence.
**`D-3`'s is `null` while its `changelog` is non-null.** The register's `_comment` states the rule in
one line: *every entry owes a changelog line that appears verbatim in `CHANGELOG.md`, **or** an
explicit `"changelog": null` with a `changelog_reason`.* D-3 is in **neither** legal state — it has
both a changelog line and a null reason, a combination the rule does not describe and the
`changelog-register-check.sh` gate does not test for (it tests presence, never coherence).

**This is not a formatting nit — it is a null parked on top of a money entry.** `D-3` is
`kind: breaking`, `classes: [effects.usage, body]`, scoped to three named `billing|`/`ledger|` cells.
Its own rationale carries an `AUDIT NOTE 2026-09-21` concluding, verbatim: *"this entry's changelog
line … is not true of the endpoint these three cells exercise today"* — because `GET /admin/usage`
still derives spend through `CostModel::derive_spend_micros` (reprice-on-read, unchanged from 1.5.5),
parses no `as_of`, and `money_book.rs`'s own module doc says the M6 fold *"is DORMANT"*. The null
`changelog_reason` is the shape of a de-claim that was started and not finished.

> **PARK — D-3.** Root cause: the entry's changelog claim (*"a rate-card edit prices what happens
> after it, not what happened before it"*) describes M6, which has not landed on this branch.
> Recommendation (owner, not taken here): **either** land M6 for real (wire `get_usage` through
> `HistoryView`/`Posting`/`Priced`, parse `as_of`, re-record the three cells) **or** de-claim the
> entry and its `CHANGELOG.md` Breaking bullet to describe what actually ships. Either way the
> `changelog_reason: null` must go — it is a field in no legal state.

#### ANOMALY 2 — `F-011r` is signed for a difference that no longer exists

```sh
grep -n 'pub at:' -B 3 crates/busbar-kernel/src/admin/v1/contract/mod.rs
# -> :422  "1.6.0 owner rule: a 1.5.5 client reading this view must see this field byte-for-byte,
#           so it is RESTORED alongside `phase`/`fires_at`."
# -> :423  pub at: Option<&'static str>,
```

The entry's id **is** its premise: *"hook write readback: `at` gives way to `fires_at`"*. `at` does
not give way. It is present on `HookView`, carries **no** `skip_serializing_if`, and is emitted
unconditionally by `#[derive(Serialize)]` on every read — including the four write-readbacks this
entry pins. It was restored by `3c118f729` (*"hooks: 1.5.5's plugin alias, hook-view at field and
settings persist accepted again"*), **an ancestor of HEAD**.

**What it is doing instead.** `F-011r` is `kind: breaking` and grants `effects.readback` — the class
the judge weights **10** — over `admin.ops|{PostHooks,PutHooksName,PatchHooksNameSettings,DeleteHooksName}|ok`.
That is the **entire persistence proof of all four admin hook writes**, held open by a signature whose
stated reason is false. Anything that changes what those four writes read back reports ACCEPTED.

> **PARK — F-011r.** Root cause: `3c118f729` fixed the divergence; the sibling entry `F-011` was
> updated to match (its rationale documents the same restoration), **`F-011r` was not**.
> Recommendation (owner, not taken here): fold the four cells' `effects.readback` into `F-011`
> (`kind: additive`) once its `additive_superset` proof is confirmed to cover `effects.readback`, or
> **delete the entry outright** — nothing is removed, so the class may need no acceptance at all.
> `CHANGELOG.md:402-405` and `:458` carry the same false break and need the same correction.

### B.1.3 A THIRD ANOMALY §7.1 DID NOT SEE — the signature does not track the scope

§7.1 recorded *"carrying a `by` (owner signature): 41/41 ✅"* and stopped there. **Presence of a
signature is not validity of a signature.** `by:` is a single flat string with no per-edit
provenance, and the scope it signs has been widened underneath it:

```sh
# replay every commit that touched the register; report kind/classes changes
# that happened WITHOUT the `by` string changing
python3 - <<'PY'
import json,subprocess
rows=[]
for line in subprocess.run(['git','log','--format=%h %ad %s','--date=short','--',
        'testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout.split('\n'):
    if not line.strip(): continue
    h=line.split()[0]
    b=subprocess.run(['git','show',f'{h}:testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'],
                     capture_output=True,text=True).stdout
    try: d=json.loads(b)
    except Exception: continue
    rows.append((line,{e['id'].split()[0]:(e['kind'],tuple(sorted(e.get('classes',[]))),e['by']) for e in d['accepted']}))
rows.reverse(); prev=None
for line,m in rows:
    if prev:
        for k in set(m)&set(prev):
            if m[k][2]==prev[k][2] and (m[k][0],m[k][1])!=(prev[k][0],prev[k][1]):
                print(k, prev[k][:2],'->',m[k][:2],'|',line[:60])
    prev=m
PY
```

**Ten kind/classes changes landed under an unchanged `by:` string.** The ones that matter:

| entry | change | commit | the `by:` it still carries |
|---|---|---|---|
| `G-1` | `improvement` → **`breaking`** *and* `[body]` → **`[body, effects.usage]`** | `48ea84225` (2026-09-21) | *"owner (Matthew Jackson), 2026-09-07 ruling"* |
| `PR2-D4` | `improvement` → `breaking`; later `+effects.egress` | `1a9c43b32`, `76e80c215` | *"…2026-09-04 PR-2 attempt-seam D-table decision"* |
| `PR2-D2` | `improvement` → `breaking` | `1a9c43b32` | *"…2026-09-04 PR-2 …"* |
| `F-003` | `improvement` → `breaking` | `7b60317fe` | *"…2026-09-04 maximum spec compliance"* |
| `S-7` | `+norm.rules` | `d0a5f8be2` | *"…2026-09-04 parity triage"* |
| `F-011` | `+effects.readback`, then removed again | `76e80c215`, `17ff5e4a2` | *"…2026-09-04 parity triage"* |

`G-1` is the sharp one: **a money class (`effects.usage`, the class the register's own rule says only
a `breaking` entry may accept) was added to a live entry by an agent**, and the field that says who
approved it still reads 2026-09-07. The promotion is *substantively* right — the entry's own
rationale had already named it as the required next step — but **the register cannot distinguish
"the owner signed this scope" from "an agent widened a scope the owner signed for something
narrower."** That is a property of the instrument, and it is why 41/41-signed is a weaker statement
than §7.1 made it sound.

A fourth, smaller instance of the same shape: `2b366e354`'s subject line reads *"F-011 / F-011b become
kind=additive … **re-stamped**"*. Diffing the two blobs shows `by:` byte-identical before and after —
**the commit that says it re-stamped the signature did not re-stamp it.**

> **PARK — the register mechanic.** Recommendation: `by:` should be a list of `{date, who, what}`
> edits, not a single string, so a scope widening cannot inherit an older approval. Until then,
> `G-1`'s agent-added `effects.usage` needs an explicit owner ruling.

### B.1.4 THE TRAP: four entries have no live subject

**Zero is the passing answer to every ban, and it is the passing answer here too.** Four of the 41
accept a difference over a set of cells the oracle never runs:

| entry | matches | owed | the finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PR2-D5` | **0** | 0 | `route.failover\|fb\|member-slow` **has never existed.** The corpus has 16 `route.failover\|*` cells (`fo\|primary-slow` is the only `slow` one, and it drives the *lane* timeout). The entry's own rationale says so and calls itself *"not scoped; unwitnessed."* |
| `M-2` | **0** | 0 | the outcome token `mid-stream-error` has never existed; the six real `stream_upstream_error` cells are `SKIP`. **The best-behaved entry in the register — it states its own deadness and means it.** |
| `S-14` | **0** | 0 | worse than empty: `^llm\|[a-z]+\|gemini\|speech\|` puts `speech` in the **obligation** slot, and all 142 five-part `llm\|` cells carry `request` there. **The regex is structurally unmatchable — a speech cell would still not match it.** And it describes replacing a `200` with an error status while `classes` is `[body, headers]`: the entry would not cover its own effect. |
| `M-1` | 1 | **0** | its one cell `billing\|key-usage\|refund-across-window` is `SKIP` in the golden — never owed, never run. |

```sh
python3 -c "
import json,re
a=json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'))['accepted']
c=[x['id'] for x in json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/cells.json'))['cells']]
print([e['id'].split()[0] for e in a if not [i for i in c if re.search(e['cells'],i)]])
"   # -> ['PR2-D5', 'M-2', 'S-14']
```

Three of the four (`PR2-D5`, `M-2`, `S-14`) declare their own emptiness in prose — which is the
honest posture and is why this is a **clean** Δ rather than a silent one. `M-1` does not. And
`S-14` is the one that is *un-closeable as written*: the day its cell lands, the entry still will not
fire.

> **PARK — S-14.** Root cause: the `cells` regex was written against a 4-slot mental model of the id
> grammar; the grammar is 5-slot with `request` fixed in position 3. Recommendation: re-scope the
> regex to the real slot layout **and** add `status` to `classes` (the entry replaces a 200 with an
> error), at the time the speech cell is recorded — not before, and not by an auditor.

**Precedent for retiring dead rows exists and is the owner's own:** five entries have already been
removed from this register by **fixing** the divergence rather than forgiving it — `D-2`, two
`F-013`s, `F-017`, `O-1` (`19550dd90`), and `F-013b` (`c77806430`, *"BOOT-043b: the undefined pool
member is refused where 1.5.5 refused it"*). Deleting a stale acceptance is a move this tree has
made six times. It is available for `F-018`, `F-011c` and `F-011r`.

### B.1.5 Mechanics that DO hold — stated so the reconciliation is not all red

```sh
python3 - <<'PY'   # whitespace-normalised, because 12 lines wrap in CHANGELOG.md
import json
a=json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-differences.json'))['accepted']
ch=' '.join(open('CHANGELOG.md').read().split())
print([e['id'] for e in a if e['changelog'] is None or ' '.join(e['changelog'].split()) not in ch])
PY
# -> []      41/41 changelog lines present verbatim
```

- **41/41** changelog lines appear verbatim in `CHANGELOG.md` (§7.1's correction of its own false
  positive re-confirmed).
- **41/41** carry a `by`.
- **41/41** `expected_cells` are exact against the live corpus.
- **41/41** are attributable to a dated owner decision — **17 distinct signing events**, the newest
  `F-019` (2026-09-22 credential-leak triage, which records in its own `by` that the agent parked it
  with cell, diff, root cause and recommendation and that the alternative was offered and rejected).
  That is what a good signature looks like, and it is the shape the other 40 should be re-stamped to.

**One evidence-chain caveat, recorded rather than hidden:** `1.6.0-register-audit.md` states its
judge source as `oracle-rust.pin release_ref=89718d8`. **`testing/shadow-oracle/oracle-rust.pin` now
reads `release_ref=ab88903`** — the pin moved after that audit was written. The corpus/ledger/width
numbers above were re-derived here against the current tree and are unaffected; any claim in that
document that quotes a *judge source line number* should be re-checked against `ab88903` before it is
relied on.

**S5 VERDICT: `IN = 41, live-and-true = 11, true-but-under-described = 12, premise-falsified = 14,
no-live-subject = 4, OUT = 1, Δ = 40 — all 40 explained. RECONCILES.** Six parks carried to the
owner: `D-3`, `F-011r`, `G-1`, `S-14`, `PR2-D5`/`M-1` (dead subjects), and the `by:`-provenance
mechanic.

## B.2 S6 — `accepted-gaps.json`. **RECONCILES, and the "documented-only" ambiguity is RESOLVED — BOTH ARE OWED.**

### B.2.1 The count

```sh
python3 -c "import json;print(len(json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/accepted-gaps.json'))['accepted']))"   # -> 3
```

| | |
|---|---|
| **COUNT IN** | **5 dormant** — 3 registered (`gemini-tool-use-token-term`, `refund-across-window`, `stream-upstream-error`) + 2 documented-only in the file's own `_comment` (`PR2-D5`, `S-14`) |
| **COUNT OUT** | **1** (M4 §11) |
| **Δ** | **4** |

**Δ = 4, and all four are explained below.** **S6 RECONCILES** — but only because the explanation is
that *none of the five enforces anything today*, which is a worse state than §7.2 described.

### B.2.2 THE FINDING §7.2 MISSED — all three registered gaps are structurally unreachable

`accepted-gaps.json`'s `_comment` names its own consumer: *"replay.sh reads this file; an id it does
not cover is RED."* That consumer exists — `bin/oracle` generates a `replay.sh` shim forwarding to
the pinned Rust judge, whose `owed_baseline_check` loads and validates the file. **But read where the
acceptance is actually applied:**

```rust
// target/oracle/rust/ab88903/checkout/crates/busbar-release-oracle/src/run.rs
let find_accept = |cid: &str| accepted.iter().find(|g| g.rx.is_match(cid));
let baseline_ids = read_ids(baseline);
...
for cid in &baseline_ids {            // <<< the ONLY loop find_accept is called from
    if owed.contains(cid) { continue; }
    ...
    match find_accept(cid) {
        Some(e) => rows.push(... "ACCEPTED named gap" ...),
        None    => rows.push(... "FAIL\towed-baseline regression" ...),
    }
}
```

**`find_accept` is reachable only for an id that is in `owed-baseline.txt`.** An accepted-gaps entry
can therefore only ever fire on a **shrink** — a cell that *was* owed at baseline and no longer is.
Now check the three:

```sh
for c in 'llm|gemini|gemini|request|ok_tool_use_tokens' \
         'billing|key-usage|refund-across-window' \
         'stream_upstream_error'; do
  printf '%-52s %s\n' "$c" "$(grep -cF "$c" testing/shadow-oracle/owed-baseline.txt)"
done
# -> llm|gemini|gemini|request|ok_tool_use_tokens        0
# -> billing|key-usage|refund-across-window              0
# -> stream_upstream_error                               0
```

**Zero. And the file says so about itself, in its own header:** *"Nothing has been dropped from the
1.5.5 golden since `owed-baseline.txt` was generated … **The entries below are cells that have NEVER
been owed.**"*

So: **all three registered accepted-gaps entries are unreachable by the only gate that reads the
file, and have been since the day they landed.** This is the same shape as the three `plane-no-money`
globs that had never matched a file, and `proto-deletion-gate.sh` greping a crate directory that no
longer exists — **a register that passes over nothing.** The entries are not *wrong*: the file's own
last sentence gives the honest reason (*"so the entry is already in place the day the cell is
baselined and then re-loses its fixture"*). They are **pre-positioned, not enforcing.** That
distinction has never been written down in a place a reader of the green run would see it.

> **PARK — the gap register's reach.** Root cause: `accepted-gaps.json` serves two purposes that the
> judge only implements one of — (a) forgive a *shrink* against `owed-baseline.txt` (implemented,
> reachable, currently matching zero rows) and (b) record *why a never-owed cell is a named SKIP*
> (not implemented anywhere; no gate reads it, no run prints it). Recommendation: either teach the
> judge to emit the (b) rows on every run so a named gap is visible in the output, or move the (b)
> entries out of the enforcement file and into a document, so the enforcement file contains only
> things that can enforce. **Not a change to make in the file.**

### B.2.3 THE "DOCUMENTED-ONLY" AMBIGUITY — RESOLVED: both are **OWED**, neither is retired

§7.2 left `PR2-D5` and `S-14` as *"2 with nowhere to be recorded."* A dormant gap that is documented
but not in the file is either **owed** or **retired**. Both are **OWED**, and here is the evidence
that decides each.

#### `PR2-D5` — **OWED.** The subject is live code; only the cell is missing.

The behaviour the gap covers is a **pool-member `attempt_timeout_ms` override taking effect on a
fallback hop**. That behaviour exists in 1.6.0 and is reached at runtime:

```sh
grep -n 'attempt_timeout_ms' crates/busbar-llm/src/engine/tables.rs crates/busbar-llm/src/engine/usage.rs
# tables.rs:132-134  "Pool-member override of the lane's `attempt_timeout_ms` (one model, different
#                     budgets per workload)"   pub(crate) attempt_timeout_ms: Option<u64>,
# usage.rs:187-195   pub(crate) fn effective_attempt_timeout_ms(...)  -> layers member over lane
```

So this is **not** green-over-nothing in the dead-subject sense: the subject is live, shipped and
**untested by the oracle in either direction** — nothing proves 1.5.5 ignored the override and
nothing would catch 1.6.0 applying it wrongly. The gap is real and it is **closeable**, at a named
cost the entry already states: a fallback-pool member override in `oracle-config.sh`, a new cell, and
a fresh 1.5.5 recording.

**One thing that makes it owed rather than schedulable here:** `oracle-config.sh` is **not in this
repo.** It exists only inside the pinned tool (`target/oracle/rust/ab88903/tool/oracle-config.sh`),
i.e. in `GetBusbar/busbar-release`. Closing `PR2-D5` is **cross-repo work**, which is exactly what
*"out of the oracle's hands"* means — and it is why it must be on a list somebody owns, not left as a
sentence in a `_comment`.

> **VERDICT: OWED.** Belongs on the master list as a cross-repo item: *"record
> `route.failover|fb|member-slow` — needs an `oracle-config.sh` member override in
> `GetBusbar/busbar-release`, a new cell, and a 1.5.5 recording."* It is not retired: the behaviour
> it would witness ships in 1.6.0.

#### `S-14` — **OWED.** The speech surface is live; the gap is real; the *entry* is unfit.

```sh
sed -n '255,290p' crates/busbar-llm-codec/src/gemini/handler.rs
# -> "Gemini speech (TTS) — `models/{id}:generateContent` with `responseModalities: [AUDIO]`"
#    read_speech_request / read_speech_response / write_speech_request  — all present
ls -d crates/busbar-voice crates/busbar-voice-codec      # -> both present
python3 -c "
import json
c=json.load(open('testing/shadow-oracle/cells.json'))['cells']
print([x['id'] for x in c if 'speech' in x['id'] or 'audio' in x['id']])"   # -> []
```

The gemini speech leaf is **served code** — reader, writer and the `Malformed` refusal the entry
describes are all present, and `busbar-voice`/`busbar-voice-codec` are still in the tree (#19's
deletion has not landed). **The corpus has zero speech cells.** A live served surface with no cell is
the definition of an owed gap, not a retired one.

What is *also* true, and is the reason this cannot simply be moved into `accepted-gaps.json` as-is:
`S-14`'s regex could not match a speech cell even after one is recorded (§B.1.4), and its `classes`
do not cover the status change it describes.

> **VERDICT: OWED**, with a precondition. Belongs on the master list as: *"record a gemini speech
> cell — needs a fixture, a pinned-mock speech verb, and the product's refusal-envelope decision"*,
> **and** `S-14`'s regex + `classes` must be corrected by the owner before that cell lands, or the
> acceptance will not fire on it. Not retired: the surface ships.

### B.2.4 The signature defect §7.2 named, re-confirmed

All three registered gaps carry `owner` (`llm-plane`, `billing`, `llm-plane`) — **a team, not a
person — and no `by` field at all.** The judge enforces only presence of `cells`/`owner`/`rationale`;
it has no concept of a signature. **#59** is explicit: *"Adding an entry to the accepted-gaps register
ALWAYS requires explicit owner approval — an agent may NEVER self-approve a gap under the owner's
name (same bar as a money-byte divergence)."* **All three are owed a sign-off they do not carry, and
the file has no field to carry it in.** Unchanged from §7.2; re-confirmed against the current pin.

**S6 VERDICT: `IN = 5 dormant (3 registered + 2 documented-only), OUT = 1, Δ = 4` — all four
explained: 3 registered-but-structurally-unreachable (pre-positioned, enforcing nothing today),
2 documented-only and now **RULED OWED** with the evidence above. **RECONCILES.**** Two parks: the
gap register's reach, and the three missing #59 signatures.

# APPENDIX C — S2b AND S2c RECONCILED (2026-09-23)

> **What this appendix settles.** §2 left **S2b** at `Δ ≈ 30` and **S2c** at `Δ ≈ 43`, both
> ❌ UNRECONCILED, each carrying two COUNT IN numbers that measure different populations. Both are
> now **Δ = 0** on every denominator they carry, under §0's own standard: *count-in = count-out, or
> every non-zero remainder explained in prose.* Every item is carried to the master list as a row
> somebody owns, shown already closed with the command that proves it, or named as real open work.
>
> **Neither published COUNT IN reproduced at PIN.** That is the first finding, and §0 demands it be
> said rather than patched: *"A number you cannot reproduce at `PIN` is a bug in this document. Say
> so."* S2b's `33` is **25**; S2c's `44` is **42**.

## C.0 THE TWO EXTRACTION BUGS, NAMED

Every census in this appendix is taken from the git object store at `PIN`, never the working tree.

```sh
PIN=016422be7904d9ede08c99dd78de855613b3da16

# S2b — no heading regex yields 33. The 33 is unreproducible.
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md    | grep -cE '^#{2,3} '   # -> 25   (published: 33)
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md    | grep -cE '^#+'        # -> 26
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md    | grep -cE '^## '       # -> 6
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md    | grep -cE '^### '      # -> 19
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md    | grep -cE '^\|'        # -> 86   (published: 86 ✓)

# S2c — the 44 IS reproducible, which is what lets the bug be named exactly.
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-duplication-census.md | grep -cE '^#{2,3} '   # -> 42   (published: 44)
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-duplication-census.md | grep -cE '^#'         # -> 44   <-- THE BUG
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-duplication-census.md | grep -cE '^\|'        # -> 264  (published: 264 ✓)

# the two lines `^#` catches that are not headings:
git show $PIN:docs/design/1.6.0-duplication-census.md | grep -nE '^#' | grep -vE '^[0-9]+:#{2,3} '
# -> 1:# 1.6.0 DUPLICATION CENSUS — where the ~91,000 excess production lines ARE
# -> 277:#![allow(dead_code)]
```

**S2c's `44` counted the H1 title plus a Rust inner attribute inside a fenced code block.** A
heading census that does not skip fences is not a heading census. **S2b's `33` matches no regex over
either of the only two revisions the file has ever had** — it is not drift, it is a number with no
provenance.

> **⚠ THE `33` VERDICT IS RIGHT AND THIS PARAGRAPH'S REASON INVITES A RE-OPEN. THE REAL ONE, WRITTEN
> DOWN SO THE NEXT AUDITOR DOES NOT HAVE TO FIND IT AGAIN.** `33` **is** the section count of
> `1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md` **today**, so *"matches no regex"* reads as false to anyone who runs the
> command against HEAD instead of `PIN`, and the obvious next hypothesis — **pin drift** — has to be
> excluded rather than assumed. It was checked and it does not hold:
>
> ```sh
> F=docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md
> for s in a0b808b79 bb83912d5 5e2fc1fad 72c9c14d6; do
>   printf '%s ' $s; git show ${s}:$F | grep -cE '^#{2,3} '; done
> # -> a0b808b79  25    2026-09-22 16:48  <-- the commit that FIRST wrote 33 INTO THIS DOCUMENT
> # -> bb83912d5  32    2026-09-22 18:18
> # -> 5e2fc1fad  33
> # -> 72c9c14d6  33    HEAD
> # CONTROL: the same grep at PIN -> 25 (non-empty), and '^\|' at PIN -> 86.
> ```
>
> **`33` was published at 16:48, when every revision of the file that had ever existed carried 25.**
> The file then **grew into the number** — 25 → 32 → 33 — over the next four hours. **Drift runs the
> wrong way for a drift explanation: a stale reading is a number the source *used* to have, and 33
> is a number the source did not have *yet*.** That is exactly why *"no provenance"* is the correct
> verdict — **there was no revision to take it from** — and the present-day coincidence is
> retrospective, not causal. (App. H.4.1.)

**Neither row count is an obligation count, and the reason is structural, not stylistic.** Sections
and rows are not disjoint populations: every table sits *inside* a section already counted. `33 + 86`
would assert 119 obligations over a 633-line document. The pipe-line counts are raw markdown,
markup included:

| | tables | header | separator | **data rows** | `^\|` total |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| **S2b** | 8 | 8 | 8 | **70** | **86** |
| **S2c** | 24 | 24 | 24 | **216** | **264** |

```sh
# reproduces both rows of that table, fence-aware (the naive ^\| does not skip code blocks)
python3 - <<'PY'
import re, subprocess
PIN="016422be7904d9ede08c99dd78de855613b3da16"
for tag,f in (("S2b","1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md"),("S2c","1.6.0-duplication-census.md")):
    blob=subprocess.run(["git","show",f"{PIN}:docs/design/{f}"],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout
    t=h=s=d=0; cur=None; fence=False
    for l in blob.split("\n"):
        if l.startswith("```"): fence=not fence; continue
        if fence: continue
        if l.startswith("|"):
            if cur is None: cur="h"; t+=1; h+=1
            elif cur=="h" and re.match(r"^\|[\s:|-]+\|?\s*$",l): cur="b"; s+=1
            else: cur="b"; d+=1
        else: cur=None
    print(tag,"tables",t,"header",h,"sep",s,"data",d,"total",h+s+d)
PY
# -> S2b tables 8  header 8  sep 8  data 70  total 86
# -> S2c tables 24 header 24 sep 24 data 216 total 264
```

## C.1 S2b `1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md` — **RECONCILES. Δ = 0.**

### C.1.1 THE BLOCK

```
S2b  docs/design/1.6.0-oracle-coverage.md
IN  = 25 §-sections   (published 33 is an extraction bug with no provenance — C.0)
      DONE 4 + OPEN 7 + NOT-AN-ITEM 14 = 25          Δ = 0
rows: IN = 86 pipe lines = 8 header + 8 separator + 70 data rows
      70 data rows absorbed, each into exactly one of the 25 sections above
                                                      Δ = 0
```

**Why COUNT OUT looked like 3.** COUNT OUT measured *"how many reached the master list as a row
somebody owns"*, which is a different question from *"is the thing it describes still owed"*. Three
of the four discharged sections were discharged **in the tree** — by a gate, by the register's own
data, and by the paragraph that was itself the deliverable — and needed no master-list row at all.
This document's own status line settles its unit: *"MEASUREMENT. Read-only."* It opens no work queue.

### C.1.5 TWO CORRECTIONS THIS RE-VERIFICATION OWES THE §6 LEDGER

Both are errors in the reconciliation itself, found by re-running its own evidence. Neither moves Δ.

1. **§1.8's figure is wrong, and in the worse direction.** The §6 ledger reads *"§3 still reads
   `UNMAPPED` for 30 of its 41 sections."* Measured:
   ```sh
   awk 'NR>=120 && NR<=172' docs/design/1.5.5-BEHAVIOUR.md | grep -cE '^\|'              # -> 42 pipe lines
   awk 'NR>=120 && NR<=172' docs/design/1.5.5-BEHAVIOUR.md | grep -E '^\|' | grep -c UNMAPPED   # -> 33
   ```
   **42 pipe lines = 1 header + 1 separator + 40 data rows; 33 are `UNMAPPED`, 7 are `PARTIAL`/`CELL`.**
   The correct figure is **33 of 40**, not 30 of 41.

2. **Item 7 is worse than "no cell" — a design binding cites a test that does not exist.**
   ```sh
   grep -rn 'a_spend_past_the_reservation_is_carried_out_as_an_overdraft' --include='*.rs' crates/ xtask/
   # -> crates/busbar/tests/money_never_ends_an_admitted_unit.rs:30   (a doc comment)
   # -> xtask/src/gates/design_bindings/tables.rs:606                 (prose inside a binding string)
   ```
   **Two hits, neither a `fn`.** `tables.rs:606` is binding **PB-58**, and it cites this name as one
   of the two tests that carry *"the behavioural half"* of a money binding. The behaviour does have
   a witness under a different name —
   `crates/busbar-kernel/tests/loop_battery.rs:486 fn running_past_the_reservation_posts_the_overdraft_rather_than_refusing()`
   — so the likely fix is to re-point PB-58's citation at the test that exists rather than to write
   a new one. **Not done here: `tables.rs` is a design-binding table, and re-pointing a binding's
   named witness is a ruling, not an edit.** **PARK — PB-58.**
   - **cell:** `xtask/src/gates/design_bindings/tables.rs:606`, binding `PB-58`.
   - **diff:** cites `busbar-llm/src/unit/meter.rs::a_spend_past_the_reservation_is_carried_out_as_an_overdraft`; that symbol is a `fn` in no file in the tree.
   - **root cause:** the binding names its witness by spelling, and nothing joins the spelling to a real `fn`, so the citation rotted silently.
   - **recommendation:** re-point at `loop_battery.rs:486`, **or** add a gate that fails when a design binding names a test symbol that does not resolve. The second closes the class, not the instance.

### C.2.4 THE ONE CLOSED ROW — PROVEN BY RUNNING THE GATE, NOT BY READING IT

```sh
cargo test -p busbar --test net_guard_one_judge
# -> running 5 tests
#    test the_dialing_arm_and_the_config_arm_read_the_same_metadata_list ... ok
#    test the_bare_authority_arm_is_reached_through_the_kernel_name ... ok

grep -rn 'METADATA_HOSTS' --include='*.rs' crates/ | grep -E 'const|static'
# -> crates/busbar-kernel-egress/src/trust/net.rs:75:pub const METADATA_HOSTS: &[&str] = &[   (ONE definition)

One definition, one shim, the parity test replaced by a one-judge test, and the positive control
`the_hostile_table_actually_reaches_the_guard_through_both_names` is what stops an all-`None` judge
passing the equalities. **This is the shape a "DONE" claim has to have.**

### C.2.5 THE FIVE REMAINING TIER-1 ITEMS — RE-VERIFIED STILL LIVE, 2026-09-23

Tier 1 is *"SAFETY AND MONEY. Small, urgent."* Every one was resolved against the tree by identifier,
not by path.

| # | row | command | verdict |
|--:|---|---|---|
| **2** | **M1** — money clamp | `grep -nE 'fn nanos\|u64::MAX as f64' crates/busbar-kernel/src/cost.rs` → `138: fn nanos`, `140: if v.is_finite() && v > 0.0 {` — **no `u64::MAX` term**; `crates/busbar-kernel-ledger/src/cost/rate.rs:42` has `v <= u64::MAX as f64` | **OPEN.** `f64 as u64` saturates, so a rate-card typo with too many zeros yields `u64::MAX` nanos/token in the **enforcement** pricer and `0` in the ledger |
| **3** | **S10** — SigV4 | `grep -rn 'fn canonicalize_header_value' --include='*.rs' crates/` → `busbar-substrate-values/src/sigv4.rs:146` **and** `busbar-kernel-identity/src/egress_auth/sigv4/mod.rs:110` | **OPEN.** Two definitions; a quoted header value signs to a different signature on the two paths |
| **4** | **S11** — token/principal | `grep -rn 'struct CallerToken\|struct Principal' --include='*.rs' crates/` → `CallerToken` at `busbar-kernel-identity/src/carrier.rs:42` **and** `crates/api/src/auth.rs:69`; `Principal` at `busbar-kernel-identity/src/principal.rs:26` **and** `crates/api/src/auth.rs:16` | **OPEN.** `crates/api` still exists; redact one `Debug` and the other still prints the bearer token |
| **5** | **S12** — breaker panic | `crates/busbar-kernel/src/store/in_memory/breaker.rs:556-564` is `jittered.clamp((duration / 2).max(1), cfg.max_cooldown_secs)` — **no `.min()` on the floor**; `crates/busbar-kernel-breaker/src/cell.rs:383` is `let floor = (duration / 2).max(1).min(cfg.max_cooldown_secs);` | **OPEN.** `clamp` panics when `min > max`, and the copy **on the serving path** is the one without the guard |
| **6** | **ENG1** — SSE grammar | `crates/busbar-a2a/src/a2a/grpc.rs:717` still `const TERMINATORS: [&[u8]; 3]`; `crates/busbar-mcp/src/mcp/client/transport.rs:416` still `.lines()`; **and see the citation drift below** | **OPEN** |

**ENG2's standing pattern, which these keep proving: a pre-switch copy that is ahead of the live one
is not dormant — it is a fix nobody shipped.** S12 is that exactly, on the serving path.

### C.2.7 THREE CORRECTIONS THIS RE-VERIFICATION OWES. NONE MOVES Δ.

1. **⚠ THE CENSUS BLOB WAS NOT BYTE-IDENTICAL AT PIN, AND THE §13 LEDGER SAYS IT WAS.** §13.1 asserts
   *"both blobs are byte-identical at the pin, so this is not drift."* True for S2b; **false for S2c**:
   ```sh
   git rev-parse 016422be7:docs/design/1.6.0-duplication-census.md   # -> ef817151c8567d6e3ffb033966cfa1680cae57ca
   git rev-parse bb83912d5^:docs/design/1.6.0-duplication-census.md  # -> a7c7d4d19ccb5b1a3634ecf0b6ea5d651317038c
   ```
   `eb750e272` changed it between PIN and the reconciliation (**+13 lines**: §3's `CLOSED` banner,
   §4.5's *"now a single implementation"*, and Tier-1 row 1 struck to `DONE`). **The arithmetic is
   unaffected — sections and pipe lines are 42/264 on both blobs** — so Δ still holds, but the
   *reason* given for it was wrong and the fix that closed ranked row 1 is exactly what moved the file.

2. **ENG1's primary citation has rotted — the symbol moved, the defect did not.**
   `crates/busbar-plane-llm/src/codec.rs` **does not exist.** Following the identifier:
   ```sh
   grep -rn 'fn split_event' --include='*.rs' crates/   # -> crates/busbar-plane-llm/src/plane.rs:275
   ```
   and both halves of the defect are intact at the new home: `for line in text.lines()` (**does not
   split on a bare CR**) and `data = rest.trim_start().as_bytes()` — an **assignment, not an
   accumulation**, so a multi-line `data:` payload still silently drops all but its last line.
   **ENG1 should read `crates/busbar-plane-llm/src/plane.rs:275`**; its sibling citations have
   drifted by lines only (`mcp/client/transport.rs:414` → `:416`, `a2a/grpc.rs:716` → `:717`).

3. **ENG26 is still open and is the one item this reconciliation *created*.** The census's §4.1 and
   §4.4 were overtaken by the fix for §3 and still read as current — and since the census is now
   deleted, the correction has nowhere to land but the row itself. **§4.1's enabler sentence is the
   one measured false in C.2.6**, and ENG2 (§11 ranked #7) quotes that same sentence as its
   structural enabler. **Correcting ENG26 means re-stating ENG2's rationale**, which is why it is a
   row and not a typo.

*Measured 2026-09-23 on `consolidated/1.6.0`. The **row set** is read from the object store at

fail — is read from **today's tree**, because vacuity is a property of the tree, not of the ledger.*

**§6 recorded S4 at IN 2,444 · OUT 2 · Δ 2,442 · ❌ UNRECONCILED.** Under SS0 the failure is the
*unexplained* delta, not the delta. Every one of the 2,444 rows now lands in exactly one bucket and
the buckets sum to the count in. **The answer is not "2,442 owed".**

| bucket | test | rows |
|---|---|---|
| **(a) LOCKED** | subject alive, row passes, nothing to pay down | **122** |
| **(b) HELD-RED** | breached by a gate today, or awaiting a named owner's ruling | **28** |
| **(c) DEAD SUBJECT** | **the crate, path, script or kind it names is not in the tree** | **62** |

| **(f) HEADROOM** | ceiling above any value the tree can produce | **509** |
| **(g) NOT-A-ROW** | template, fixture, rule's own spec; ~~+ the 2 phantom rows of §6.3~~ **— the 2 phantom rows are STRUCK, not bucketed (App. H.6.1)** | ~~**131**~~ → **129** |
| | ~~122+28+62+1,543+49+509+131~~ → **122+28+62+1,543+49+509+129** | ~~2,444~~ → **2,442 — Δ = 0** |

> **THE PARTITION IS SOUND AND THE HEADLINE WAS NOT.** Bucket (g) carried the two phantom rows of
> §6.3 in six words, `Δ = 0` closed on them, and **§6.3 did not mention them at all** — so the only
> place in this document that knew COUNT IN was 2 rows high was a parenthesis inside the appendix
> that depended on it. Corrected in both places on 2026-09-23.
>
> **And these seven buckets are a classification of the source's own rows, not a COUNT OUT.** None
> of (a)–(g) is *"reached the master list"*; bucket **(d) is 1,543 rows of live open work with no
> map row at all**. **§6.4's `COUNT OUT = 2` — *"Only two reach the master list: M1 §3 names
> `INSTANCE-NOUN` and `KIND-ISOLATION`"* — was never withdrawn and is still the figure §0 asks
> for.** Stated as §0 defines the terms: **IN 2,442 · OUT 2 · Δ 2,440** (App. H.6.4).

**✅ S4 RECONCILES** — not because the work is done (**1,543 rows are real open work**) but because
every row is classified and the classification is reproducible. The full derivation, the 62 named
dead subjects, the bucket rules and the runnable command are in
[`1.6.0-ratchet-census.md` §R.11](./1.6.0-ratchet-census.md#r11-the-audit-partition--seven-buckets-one-command--0),
which sub-partitions §R.3's four buckets so that `(a)=SATISFIED`, `(b)+(d)=OWED`,
`(c)+(e)+(f)=VACUOUS`, `(g)=NOT-A-ROW`.

## D.1 The finding: 62 ratchets that cannot produce a NO

**A ratchet whose subject no longer exists is not a check.** Nine deleted crates account for 53 of
them; the rest are absent scripts and absent test targets:

| dead subject | rows | dead subject | rows |
|---|---|---|---|
| `busbar-admin` | 14 | `busbar-plane-a2a-host` | 3 |
| `busbar-plugin-sign` | 8 | `busbar-plane-mcp-host` | 3 |
| kind `grammar` (no live member) | 6 | `busbar-core` | 3 |
| `busbar-plugin-pack` | 5 | `crates/plugin-sign/` | 1 |
| `busbar-substrate` | 4 | 6 absent scripts (`full-gate` `[[skip]]`) | 6 |
| `busbar-grammar` named directly | 3 | 3 absent test targets (`segments`) | 3 |
| `busbar-plane-admin` | 3 | | |
| | | **TOTAL** | **62** |

**The `kind-isolation` gate already agrees, out loud and in red**, naming this class itself today:
`dead-cell` ×12, `dead-dep-edge` ×25, `dead-edge` ×18, `dead-kind` ×1, `alias-retired` ×1. Its own
rule is already written — *"a row whose cell measures zero is a dead allowance and must be struck"*.

**The sharpest single row: the leak moved and the ledger did not.** `instance-noun-neutrality.toml`
pins `noun = "valkey", count = 36` to `crates/plugin-sign/src/tests/lib_tests.rs`, a deleted crate.
All 36 occurrences are in `crates/plugin-loader/src/tests/sign_tests.rs` today — **which has no row
at all.** That row must be *repointed*, not struck.

## D.2 Reproducing it

The dead-subject bucket is **mechanical** — crate, path, script and kind existence, no gate and no
judgement. The script is in `1.6.0-ratchet-census.md` §R.11.2 and prints the bucket counts; the
three gate invocations beneath it produce buckets (b) and (f):

```sh
./target/debug/xtask gate plane-purity-strict --format=tsv   # (f) 508 lines of headroom
./target/debug/xtask gate construction        --format=tsv   # (b) the breached ratchets
./target/debug/xtask gate kind-isolation      --format=tsv   # the gate's own dead-row findings
```

Part **D** reproduces exactly from the first: `SYMBOL 9 ≤ 61`, `TYPE 0 ≤ 13`, `KEY 139 ≤ 377`,
`DIALECT 312 ≤ 456`, `BACKWARDS 0 ≤ 33`, `test-reach llm/mcp/a2a 0 ≤ 24/1/3` — **owed 460,
headroom 508.** Part **C** reproduces exactly from the second: `legacy-reach` 17/17,
`one-teller-loop:run_gauntlet` 4/4, `sealed-unit-traits` 1/1, `max_callers_per_plane_crate` 0 across
all four plane crates (the one vacuous permitted site), and **two breaches** —
`no-test-doubles-in-production:doubles` **7 against ratchet 5** and `one-pick-site` **4 against
ceiling 2**.

**Two false zeros were hit and are guarded by assertions in the script.** A crate token may be a
**package name or a directory name** (`crates/api` is `busbar-api`) — testing directory-spelled
tokens against package names alone reported **9 live crates as dead**. And the kind matcher
`*-codec` is a **suffix**, not a prefix (`matcher_hit`, `xtask/src/gates/kind_isolation.rs:1453`) —
reading it as a prefix reported kind `codec` as member-less while both `busbar-llm-codec` and
`busbar-voice-codec` are in the tree.

## D.3 Corrections to §6, and the parks

- **§6.5(c) is now FIVE authorities for one quantity, not four.** `construction` measures
  `busbar-contract` at **6,815** today (`loc-ceilings:caps-contract` FAIL and `surface-ceiling:contract`
  FAIL, both against ceiling 5,652), beside the 5,652 / 6,659 / 7,012 / 7,426 already tabulated.
- **§6.5(b) is settled and was a false alarm about the tree.** `construction`'s
  `ports-only-tests.max_per_crate` and `plane-purity-strict`'s `[test-reach]` both measure **0** for
  llm/mcp/a2a/voice. The instruments agree; what is stale is `plane-purity-strict`'s own **ceilings**
  (24/1/3 over a measurement of 0) and its comment citing a figure `construction.toml` does not carry.
- **§6.3's Part B under-counts by 14.** `rules.no-test-doubles-in-production.known_sites` is a TOML
  **table of 14 records**, not an array, so an array-walking extraction never saw it. Five carry
  `verdict = "double"` and are already counted in Part C; **9 carry `verdict = "real"` and are counted
  nowhere.** The honest COUNT IN is **2,453**. This appendix reconciles the **2,444 on the record** and
  records the 9 as a remainder — moving a source's count-in to make it reconcile is the move SS0 refuses.
- **The instrument for bucket (f) exists and is blind to the biggest pile.** `construction`'s
  `ceiling-slack` row — *"a ceiling above its measurement is room nobody voted for"* — is FAIL today
  and reports exactly **one** slack ceiling. It does not read `plane-purity-strict.toml`, where **508
  lines** of the same slack sit in the next file.
- **Two red-ability proofs are themselves vacuous** (`xtask/src/gates/kind_isolation.rs:5795`, `:5810`).
  Both plant `Overlay::remove` on a `Cargo.toml` that is **already absent** (`busbar-grammar`,
  `busbar-plane-voice`), and `kind-isolation:registry` is **already RED naming the exact tokens they
  assert**. `prove_red` (`xtask/src/gates/mod.rs:1828`) runs the gate **only with the plant** and never
  establishes a green baseline, so a no-op plant over an already-red row passes. **This is the defect
  class the release is about, inside the proof machinery itself.**

Eight parks — P1 the 62 dead subjects, P2 `contract_caps`, P3 `ceiling-slack` scope, P4 the two
red-ability proofs, P5 the Part B extraction, P6/P7 the two breached ratchets, P8 the 169 unlisted
`kind-isolation` subjects — are tabulated in `1.6.0-ratchet-census.md` §R.11.5. **None was actioned.**

> **What changed in the tree for this appendix:** the §2 S4 row, ~~the §2 tally (8 → 9 of 14)~~ **(struck —
> wrong denominator; §2 now derives the tally by command)**, the §11
> S4 summary row, this section, and `1.6.0-ratchet-census.md` §R.11. **Every `qa/*.toml` was opened
> read-only — no ratchet value, waiver, ceiling or glob was changed and no row was struck.** No
> golden, no `accepted-differences.json` and no `accepted-gaps.json` was written or regenerated. The
> three gates invoked were run unmodified from the prebuilt `target/debug/xtask`.

# APPENDIX E — S3 RECONCILED: THE CORPUS RE-WALKED AT 452, AND THE 145 COMMITS THAT LANDED AFTER THE READ

> **Appended 2026-09-23.** §§0–12 and Appendices A–D above are other agents' work and are not
> edited here, except for the two cells §2 and §B.4 reserve for a source's status. This appendix
> answers the one thing §5 left open: **S3, the largest unreconciled source in the file** —
> *"307 commits → 242 residue findings"* IN, *"≤ 45"* OUT, **Δ ≥ 197, ❌ UNRECONCILED**.
>
> **It re-walks the corpus rather than re-citing it.** The window in §5 is an open-ended `--since`;
> it has grown by 145 commits since the read. Every body in that growth was read. Every number
> below carries the command that produces it, and **three of §5's own figures do not reproduce** —
> those are stated as defects, with the cause, rather than carried forward.
>
> **PINNED AT `890debf69`.** §0.1's hazard reproduced during this work: the tree moved under the
> measurement. See §E.9.

## E.0 THE HEADLINE, BEFORE THE DETAIL

**S3 reconciles at Δ = 0 in the unit that is enumerable on both sides — the commit — across the
full 452-commit window, and the remainder is two named open items, not 197.**

The delta was never 197 items of dropped work. It was manufactured by three things, each now
measured: **an unpinned window**, **an IN side that was never enumerated**, and — the load-bearing
one — **a COUNT OUT measured against a master list that did not exist yet.** `1.6.0-LEDGER.md` was
created at `3d822e503`, *inside the commit range S3 was analysing*. §5 was right about the document
it could see. It cannot be cited now.

**And the mechanism S3 exists to catch caught this file's own reconciliation.** The work that drove
S3 to Δ = 0 was done, in full, in `docs/design/1.6.0-commit-residue.md` — **and that document was
folded and `git rm`'d at `2deac0578` before anyone carried its verdict back to §2.** Its §11 closes
with a sentence nobody acted on: *"What must change in `1.6.0-map-proof.md` §2 and §5 — **not done
here**."* A finding recorded, never actioned, then removed for tidiness. **That is §12.3's circle,
closing for the third time in this release** (after the LOST register at `9b14f4f5a`→`a07a7591e`,
and the fold register in §E.6). This appendix is the carry-back.

## E.1 THE WINDOW, RE-DERIVED — AND §5's PRINTED COMMAND WAS NEVER A PIN

§5 prints an **open-ended** `--since` with no upper bound, so it does not measure a fixed corpus:

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' 890debf69 | sort -u | wc -l   # -> 452
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' a197ad2f5 | sort -u | wc -l   # -> 307
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' 016422be7 | sort -u | wc -l   # -> 310
```

| bound | commits | what it is |
|---|---:|---|
| `a197ad2f5` | **307** | **the corpus §5 actually read** — §5.1 names it as its own last commit |
| `016422be7` (`PIN`) | **310** | the bound §0.1 *declares* for every figure in this file |
| `890debf69` | **452** | the same command today |

**§5's 307 does not reproduce at this file's own `PIN`.** §0.1 states *"Every number below is
therefore read from the git object store at `PIN`"*; §5's is read at `a197ad2f5`, three commits
earlier. The three are named rather than rounded away:

```sh
comm -23 <(git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' 016422be7 | sort -u) \
         <(git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' a197ad2f5 | sort -u)
#   016422be7  money: one function, one test, and the table that says what the other twenty answer
#   c08cd2424  reap: the load counters used pgrep, which matched none of these paths
#   c2bb5429a  reap: match the build-artifact SHAPE, not the literal 'target/'
```

**`016422be7` is the `PIN` itself, and it carries residue** — *"The conversions are PARKED
(#10/#59) and reported."* **The commit this file pins every number to was never inside the corpus
that file's largest source claims to have read end to end.** It is bucketed in §E.5.2.

**Correction to the premise that commissioned this work:** the growth is **145 commits, not ~370**.
`452 − 307 = 145`, and `git log --since=… a197ad2f5..890debf69 | wc -l` returns 145 directly.

## E.2 THE 242 IS NOT A COUNT, AND THAT IS SETTLED BY COMMAND

§5.1 classifies the 242 into five named classes. **None of the five is a string in the corpus:**

```sh
for L in OWED-WORK SEPARATE-FINDING KNOWN-RED-ON-PURPOSE EXEMPTION-GRANTED OWNER-RULING; do
  printf '%-22s %s\n' "$L" \
    "$(git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%b' 890debf69 | grep -cF -- "$L")"
done
#   OWED-WORK              0
#   SEPARATE-FINDING       0
#   KNOWN-RED-ON-PURPOSE   0
#   EXEMPTION-GRANTED      0
#   OWNER-RULING           0
```

**The per-class tally was a read, not a command's output, and no list of 242 exists in this tree or
any other.** Under this file's own §0 — and under the ruling at `5b3ea85b0` that struck an
unenumerated tally for exactly this reason — **242 cannot be the IN side of a join.** It is carried
below as what it is: a statement-level estimate, bridged in §E.4, never adopted.

**The residue class is semantic, not lexical, and no regex closes it.** §12.4(9) proposes a
detector vocabulary; measured over the full window it finds **140 commits** —

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'===C %h%n%s%n%b' 890debf69 \
 | awk '/^===C /{h=$2;seen=0;next} !seen&&tolower($0)~/owed|still owes|separate finding|parked|left red/{print h;seen=1}' \
 | sort -u | wc -l          # -> 140   (body only: 138; the 2 subject-only are 0f7766c13, be72bf69a)
```

— and **it is provably lossy.** `f6b663153` (*"456 of the 912 mcp cells name a method the battery
never sends"*), `9e44d8e5d` (*"the trunk's mcp handshake id is 0, which is also the first
request's"*) and `02aeefd1d` (*"the roster cannot name 19 of 29"*) are unambiguous owed work by this
file's own definition and trip **no** keyword. **The detector is reported here as a floor, never as
the counter.** The reconciliation below is therefore run at the **commit**, which is enumerable on
both sides and cannot be gamed by vocabulary.

## E.3 W-A — THE 307 §5 READ. RE-VERIFIED, AND ITS PUBLISHED SPLIT IS WRONG BY EXACTLY 11

The commit-level join for the 307 was done in `1.6.0-commit-residue.md` §11.3 and published as
**267 cited + 40 uncited**. Re-run against the blob that carries it:

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' a197ad2f5 | sort -u > /tmp/win307
git show 2deac0578^:docs/design/1.6.0-commit-residue.md \
  | grep -oE '`[0-9a-f]{7,40}`' | tr -d '`' | sort -u > /tmp/res_tokens
comm -12 /tmp/win307 /tmp/res_tokens | wc -l      # -> 278   (published: 267)
comm -23 /tmp/win307 /tmp/res_tokens | wc -l      # ->  29   (published:  40)
```

**Three published figures are wrong by the same 11** — 282→293 tokens, 267→278 cited, 40→29
uncited. **The cause is exact and the 11 are named.** §11.3's own prose cites eleven window commits
that its arithmetic was computed before: the five residue-carriers it places (`136a52776`,
`22a2c63fd`, `05d00e4dc`, `f5aa3d427`, `d1b3b28c5`) and the six it names as read-in-full
(`85d56e99c`, `3f3b4af6d`, `04dea4ac4`, `f94afa1f6`, `b91742c94`, `d5538959a`). Writing them into
the document moved each from *uncited* to *cited*; the totals were never re-run.

```sh
for h in 136a52776 22a2c63fd 05d00e4dc f5aa3d427 d1b3b28c5 \
         85d56e99c 3f3b4af6d 04dea4ac4 f94afa1f6 b91742c94 d5538959a; do
  printf '%s in_window=%s cited=%s\n' "$h" \
    "$(grep -c "^$h$" /tmp/win307)" "$(grep -c "^$h$" /tmp/res_tokens)"
done      # -> 11/11 in window, 11/11 cited
```

**267 + 11 = 278. 40 − 11 = 29. 278 + 29 = 307.** It is a defect in a printed number and it is
recorded as one — **and the reconciliation is unharmed: the split moved, the total did not, and W-A
is Δ = 0 either way.** The item-level output of that work survives the document's deletion: it is
**R1–R78** in `1.6.0-LEDGER.md`.

```sh
grep -cE '^\| \*{0,2}R[0-9]+\*{0,2} \|' docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md   # -> 78
```

**78 rows: 62 OPEN (3 status-qualified), 11 PARKED-OWNER, 4 IN-FLIGHT, 1 ANSWERED.** That is S3's
real COUNT OUT at the item level, against §5's guess of *"≤ 45 … for the whole master list"*.

## E.4 THE BRIDGE FROM 242, CARRIED FORWARD RATHER THAN RE-DERIVED

`1.6.0-commit-residue.md` §11.6 bridges §5's statement tally to an enumerated item list, each
transform with its source: `242 −32 (collapse) −12 (collapse) +12 (expansion) +30 (LOST register)
+47 (rider clauses) = 287` against **291 enumerated**, **residual +4, named** — three of which
(`A70`, `A71`, `A72`) are tree measurements that no body ever asserted and so are certainly not
among the 242. **That bridge is adopted as-is, not re-run**, and it is adopted with its own stated
limit: the residual cannot be driven to zero because the IN side was never enumerated. Under §0
that is a reconciled remainder. It is recorded here because the document carrying it is no longer
in the tree, and a reconciliation whose evidence a reader cannot open is not one.

## E.5 W-B — THE 145 COMMITS THAT LANDED AFTER THE READ. THIS IS THE NEW WORK.

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' a197ad2f5..890debf69 | sort -u | wc -l   # -> 145
```

**All 145 bodies were read.** They are 5,702 lines / 354 KB. Each is placed in exactly one bucket.

### E.5.1 T — 103 commits place their own residue, by construction

The `1.6.0-LEDGER.md` **FOLD** register defines the release's recording pipeline: an audit document
is written, its findings are folded into ledger rows, then the scratch is removed. **A commit that
writes into that pipeline in the same commit that records the finding has done the thing S3 exists
to require.** That is mechanically checkable:

```sh
PAT='docs/design/1\.6\.0-(LEDGER|MAP|TRACKER|map-proof|commit-residue|owner-decisions|register-audit|conformance-rig-audit|docs-inventory|done-readout|dormant-tests-audit|duplication-census|engine-split-plan|enrolment-audit|gate-sweep|instrument-audit|local-only-audit|map-s5-s6-s10|money-census|oracle-coverage|oracle-skip-audit|ratchet-census|shadowed-guard-audit|surface-drift-audit|branch-audit-[a-z]+)\.md|docs/design/BUSBAR-1\.6\.0\.md'
for h in $(git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%h' a197ad2f5..890debf69); do
  git show --name-only --pretty=format: $h | grep -qE "^($PAT)$" && echo "T $h" || echo "X $h"
done | sort | uniq -c -w1
#   103 T      42 X
```

**Not one of the 103 is a bare touch.** Every one adds at least one line to a tracked document, and
the aggregate is **26,799 lines**:

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --numstat --pretty=format: a197ad2f5..890debf69 \
 | grep -E "	($PAT)$" | awk '{s+=$1} END{print s}'      # -> 26799
```

### E.5.2 X — the 42 that do not, adjudicated one at a time

| bucket | n | how it was resolved |
|---|---:|---|
| **(a) CITED by a live master-list row** | **15** | full sha resolves in `1.6.0-{LEDGER,MAP,TRACKER}.md` |
| **(b) WROTE an audit document since folded into the ledger by row id** | **6** | the fold commit names the rows; §E.6 |
| **(c) PLACED on re-check, by row or by live test** | **3** | named below |
| **(d) CLOSED inside the window by a named following commit** | **4** | closing sha printed |
| **(e) CLOSED by the commit itself — fix-only, nothing owed** | **10** | red-before-green in the body |
| **(f) records a correction or tooling; residue explained in place** | **2** | `2a16e3309`, `bfbd2ae73` |
| **(g) OPEN, UNPLACED** | **2** | **§E.7** |
| | **42** ✅ | |

**(a), the 15** — `7265a533b` `9e8dea58b` `211ecd799` `c29dedb1c` `4e39e1a11` `653c2d9dd`
`90e4586a9` `81ae8cd02` `66627c65a` `c6951e1f8` `e8ec4eaee` `87174f503` `52a98a9f6` `bbe4ab887`
`05e57c51a`.

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --pretty=format:'%H' a197ad2f5..890debf69 | sort -u > /tmp/wb
grep -oE '\b[0-9a-f]{7,40}\b' docs/design/1.6.0-{LEDGER,MAP,TRACKER}.md | sort -u \
 | while read t; do git rev-parse -q --verify "$t^{commit}"; done | sort -u > /tmp/cited
comm -12 /tmp/wb /tmp/cited | wc -l      # -> 15
```

**(c), the 3 — each proved against the tree by symbol, never by path:**

* **`016422be7`** — *"The conversions are PARKED (#10/#59) and reported."* Placed **twice**: ledger
  row **`R7`** (`grep -n 'money-one-function' docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md` → `:268`, status
  *"Plan landed; **HELD** behind the streaming fold"*), **and** as live red-capable tests. The nine
  disagreements `D1`–`D9` survive as named rules in
  `crates/busbar/tests/money_one_function_equivalence.rs` (40 asserts;

  (`assert_eq!(figure.micros(), 34_550, "money = f(ledger, card history)")`).
  **`git grep -nE '34_?550' -- crates/` → 6 live sites.**
* **`465d9f207`** — the surviving `no-exemplar busbar-unit-auth` deviation and its owner question

* **`891219cdf`** — *"It does not change the exit code; that is a separate ruling."* Placed:

## E.6 THE STRUCTURAL FINDING THIS RE-WALK ADDS — THE FOLD REGISTER LOST SEVEN DOCUMENT NAMES

The **(b)** bucket is six commits, and running them down found a defect in the master list itself.

Seven audit documents were written after `a197ad2f5`, folded into `1.6.0-LEDGER.md`, and deleted:
`1.6.0-unconstructed-audit` · `1.6.0-sibling-repo-map` · `1.6.0-feature-plugin-census` ·
`1.6.0-deferral-reconcile` · `1.6.0-unreachable-audit` · `1.6.0-release-engine-audit` ·
`1.6.0-uncommitted-inventory`.

**Their findings reached rows. Their names did not.** The fold bullets that absorbed them were
written with **no subject at all**:

```sh
awk 'NR>=765 && NR<=772{printf "%d|%s|\n", NR, $0}' docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md
#   765|**FOLDED AND DELETED (24):**|
#   766|* `1.6.0-register-audit` → M6–M17, S4, S5, G1–G5, T1–T9, DOC1, DOC2.|
#   767|*  +  → the **CAN WE SHIP** table (RB1–RB10),|
#   769|*  +  → the live-harm rows, S29–S35,|
#   772|*  → M44, M45, M52, S26–S28, ENG27, G37, G38, G39, G51–G54.|
```

Three bullets carry an arrow and a row list with nothing on the left of it. The `+` in two of them
is the residue of *"`A` + `B` →"*, so **five** names were dropped in those three lines alone.
Traced to the commit that did it:

```sh
git show 93472be40 -- docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md | grep -E '^[-+].*(FOLDED AND DELETED|\*  \+|release-engine-audit|sibling-repo-map)'
#   -**FOLDED AND DELETED (17):**        +**FOLDED AND DELETED (22):**
#   +*  +  → the **CAN WE SHIP** table (RB1–RB10),
#   -**LANDED, NOT YET FOLDED:** `1.6.0-release-engine-audit` · … `1.6.0-uncommitted-inventory` …
```

The names were removed from **LANDED, NOT YET FOLDED** and never written into the bullet that
replaced it. **Those documents are now in neither list.** Measured:

```sh
git log --since='2026-09-21 05:00' --diff-filter=D --name-only --pretty=format: 890debf69 \
  -- 'docs/design/1.6.0-*.md' | grep -v '^$' | sort -u | wc -l        # -> 32 deleted in the window
sed -n '/\*\*FOLDED AND DELETED/,/FOLDED DIRECT FROM/p' docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md | grep -c '^\*'        # -> 14 bullets
sed -n '/\*\*FOLDED AND DELETED/,/FOLDED DIRECT FROM/p' docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md | grep '^\*' | grep -c '1\.6\.0-'  # ->  9 name a document
```

**The header claims 24 and nine bullets name a document.** *(Extraction note, recorded rather than
quietly fixed: a naive `1\.6\.0-[a-z-]+` sweep also reports the eight `1.6.0-branch-audit-{aa..ah}`
as unnamed. They are named, collectively, by the brace form. That is my regex's error, not the
register's, and the eight are **not** in the seven above.)*

**`1.6.0-commit-residue` is the sharpest case, because it is filed in the wrong list outright.**
It was folded to **R1–R77** and `git rm`'d at `2deac0578` — and at HEAD it still sits under
**LANDED, NOT YET FOLDED** (`:816`), while **two live ledger rows (`DOC3` `:534`, `R53` `:548`)
cite `docs/design/1.6.0-commit-residue.md` as their WHERE**, a path that does not exist:

```sh
git cat-file -e HEAD:docs/design/1.6.0-commit-residue.md ; echo "rc=$?"     # -> rc=1, ABSENT
grep -n 'commit-residue' docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md                        # -> :534 :548 :756 :758 :816
```

**No work is lost — the findings are rows, and the rows are present.** All nineteen row ids the
nameless bullets claim resolve to a live table row:

```sh
for id in RB1 RB10 M46 M51 S29 S35 G48 G50 M52 M44 M45 S26 S28 ENG27 G37 G38 G39 G51 G54; do
  grep -cE "^\| \*{0,2}$id\*{0,2} \|" docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md; done | sort -u   # -> 1
```

**What is lost is provenance**, and provenance is the whole of S3. With the name gone there is no
way to ask the ledger *"did that audit's findings reach a row, and which?"* — which is the question
§12.3 says nothing in this tree can answer. **It is the same circle, one level up: the register
built to stop findings evaporating is itself evaporating the record of where they went.**

## E.7 THE TWO RESIDUALS — NAMED, WITH THE COMMAND THAT PROVES EACH OPEN

Neither is money. Neither is self-approved. Both are owed a row and have none.

### RESIDUAL 1 — four conformance cells have a resolvable owner and nothing that runs them

From **`9cbb0f9c4`**. The done script names the gap in a comment and no row owns it:

```sh
sed -n '913,919p' scripts/verify-1.6.0-done.sh
#   # and RUN in the PARITY group against the published 1.5.5 golden. That leaves FOUR named cells
#   # with a resolvable owner and no arm in this file that runs them:
#   #   mcp.battery|ADV.MALFORMED-JSON, mcp.battery|SEAM.UPSTREAM-FAILURE-IS-TOOL-ERROR
#   #     -> testing/mcp-conformance/src/suites/{server-adversarial,seam}.mjs
#   #   a2a.supplement|AUTH-SERVER-002, a2a.supplement|AUTH-SCOPE-001
for p in 'MALFORMED-JSON' 'AUTH-SCOPE-001'; do
  grep -c "$p" docs/design/1.6.0-LEDGER.md docs/design/1.6.0-MAP.md docs/design/1.6.0-TRACKER.md; done
#   -> 0 in all three, both patterns
```

**The rig column fills 50 plane × step slots; 2 are declared gaps; of the 48 named cells, 21 run
here, 23 run in the PARITY group, and 4 run nowhere.** `21 + 23 + 4 = 48`. The runners exist
(`testing/mcp-conformance/src/suites/{server-adversarial,seam}.mjs`,
`testing/a2a-supplement/run-supplement.sh`); no done-oracle arm invokes them. **A comment is where
a reader meets a gap; it is not a row that can go red.** Owed: a row, and an arm or an explicit
declared gap.

## E.11 DOES S3 RECONCILE?

**YES — Δ = 0 at the commit level across the full re-derived window, with every remainder named.**

* **452 in, 452 placed**, in nine buckets that sum exactly, with the partition command printed.
* **W-A re-verified** and three of its published figures corrected by exactly 11, the 11 named,
  the total unchanged.
* **W-B read end to end** — all 145 bodies — and bucketed, with the closing sha printed for every
  closure and the row id for every placement.
* **COUNT OUT measured at 78**, not guessed at *"≤ 45"*, against a ledger that now exists.
* **RESIDUAL = 2**, each with a file:line, a command proving it open, and a recommendation.

**And the number that matters did not improve, which is the point.** S3 does not reconcile because
the work got done. It reconciles because every commit in the window now has a bucket and every
finding a row or a name — and **62 of the 78 rows it produced are OPEN and 11 are PARKED-OWNER.**
The delta was never 197 items of debt. It was a measurement taken against a document that had not
been written, in a window that was never pinned, counting a unit that was never enumerated.

**The structural finding stands exactly where §12.3 left it, and §E.6 is fresh evidence for it.**
Every source that reconciled had a counter that refuses. S3 still has none: this appendix is the
counter, and it is a manual one. **The fold register lost seven document names between the pin and
today, and nothing went red.**

(`--since='2026-09-21 05:00'`, bounded — an unbounded `--since` is not a pin). Every command is

rather than worked around.*
